## Phase 1 — PDF ingestion and extraction (started 2026-10-12)
Scope: FR-001, FR-002, D-01 table-extraction check. Plan approved 2026-10-12.

- [x] Commit 1 — package layout (`src/credit_intel/`, `ingestion/`), `uv_build` backend, dependencies, licence notes
- [x] Commit 2 — validation and error codes (FR-001.1–.5, .7–.9); `E-INPUT-NO-TEXT-LAYER` defined, raised in commit 3
  - `ingestion/validation.py::validate_pdf` checks in order: readable → size → `%PDF-` signature → SHA-256 → parse/encryption → page count
  - Verified pdfplumber 0.11.10 behaviour before mapping errors: user-password PDFs raise `PdfminerException` wrapping `PDFPasswordIncorrect`; **owner-password-only PDFs open normally**, so encryption is detected via the document's `/Encrypt` entry; corrupt/truncated files raise `PdfminerException` wrapping `PDFSyntaxError`/`PSEOF`
  - Any exception raised by the PDF library while parsing the (untrusted) input maps to `E-INPUT-CORRUPT`; the original is kept as `__cause__` and its text is never shown to users. The broad `except` wraps only the two parsing calls (`pdfplumber.open`, page-tree load); our own code sits outside it, so programming errors surface instead of being reported as corrupt input (regression test included)
  - Known limitation: the file is opened several times (signature, hash, parse, later extraction). The SHA-256 identifies the bytes at hashing time; a file edited or replaced mid-run could be read differently by later steps. Inputs are assumed stable during a run; if enforcement is needed, read once and parse from in-memory bytes
  - Unreadable-file test simulates `PermissionError` instead of using `chmod`, which root and some containers bypass
  - Synthetic PDFs built with reportlab in pytest `tmp_path` (`tests/conftest.py`); none written to the repository
- [x] Commit 3 — page text extraction behind `TextExtractor` (FR-002.1–.5) and `E-INPUT-NO-TEXT-LAYER` (FR-001.6)
  - `TextExtractor.extract_pages()` added; pdfplumber code stays in `pdfplumber_extractor.py`
  - `ingestion/ingest.py::ingest` = `validate_pdf` → `extract_pages` → numbering check → no-text check; returns `IngestedDocument(file_info, pages)`
  - Text is stored exactly as pdfplumber's default `extract_text()` returns it (empty string for pages without text); no cleanup, so it stays usable as evidence
  - A page "has text" if it contains any non-whitespace character; the PDF is rejected only if no page has text
  - Page labels come from the PDF's `/PageLabels` metadata via pdfminer; an absent or malformed label tree gives `None` and never rejects the document
  - Python 3.12 check moved to `tests/test_package.py`; `tests/test_placeholder.py` removed
  - Limitations: (1) page labels are metadata, not printed footer numbers — many reports define none; (2) only pdfplumber's default (non-layout) text mode is used; column/table fidelity is assessed in commit 5 (D-01); (3) the former FR-001.6 "minimum share of candidate pages" clause is removed in the PRD v0.3 draft (D-03), so there is nothing further to implement for it; (4) extraction speed is not yet measured (SC-10)
- [x] Commit 4 — statement locator (FR-013) and statement-basis check (H.4.6), implemented 2026-10-13
  - `ingestion/locator.py`: `locate_statements` (rules 1–5, 7–8), `parse_page_range`, `statement_runs` (rule 6), `check_statement_basis` (H.4.6); works on `PageText` from ingestion, no second PDF parse
  - `config.LocatorSettings` holds the PRD's initial phrase lists and thresholds; new error codes `E-INPUT-PAGE-RANGE` and `E-EXTRACT-BASIS-UNAVAILABLE` (messages state what the user should do)
  - Tests: 85 new test cases (including parametrised cases) in `tests/unit/test_locator.py` (B-121–B-130); 15 deliberate bugs were introduced in a scratch copy and each made at least one test fail (two initially survived — missing title requirement and missing NFKC — and tests were added for both)
  - Owner-approved clarifications (2026-10-13): (1) a statement's basis (H.4.6) comes from its title block — the line(s) containing the title phrase, plus the line directly above or below only if it holds nothing but a basis word (e.g. "STANDALONE" / "(Consolidated)"); basis words elsewhere (company names, notes, other lines) are ignored; if the basis cannot be established it stays `unknown`, and absent consolidated evidence is never read as standalone; (2) whitespace is allowed around page-range numbers, hyphens, and commas but not inside a number (`1 - 10` valid, `1 0` invalid); (3) `LocatorResult.range_pages_without_text` is an approved additional output field: pages in the requested range without usable text, in ascending order, empty for keyword location
  - Limitations: (1) phrase lists and thresholds are **not validated on real documents** (OD-3); no statement-location accuracy is claimed; (2) `E-EXTRACT-NO-STATEMENT-PAGES` is not raised here — the locator returns an empty result and FR-003.7 (Phase 2) raises it; (3) settings are not yet loaded from `config/settings.toml`, and `--pages`/`--basis` are not wired to a CLI (FR-014, Phase 5); (4) the manifest and memo listing of range pages without text depends on FR-012/FR-009 (Phase 5); (5) a basis word on a separate line is recognised only when that line is directly adjacent to the title and contains nothing else; other layouts give basis `unknown`
- [x] B-120 — fresh-process golden-output test for page extraction (FR-002.3), implemented 2026-10-17 (added together with this entry)
  - `tests/unit/test_fresh_process_golden.py` builds a synthetic 4-page PDF at test time with the existing `make_pdf` helper (reportlab, `invariant=1`; no PDF is committed), runs `ingest()` in a separate interpreter (`sys.executable -I -c`), and compares the child's stdout byte for byte with `tests/fixtures/page_extraction_golden.json`
  - Golden design: rows of `[page_number, page_label, text]` as ASCII-only JSON (non-ASCII shown as `\uXXXX`) so a diff shows the exact character. The PDF has a blank page 2, labels i, ii, 1, 2, a three-line page and a page with non-ASCII text (`Café – naïve résumé`). The golden was generated from the real extractor and reviewed by eye against the input strings before acceptance. A second test checks that the golden still contains the blank page, the labels, multi-line text and non-ASCII text
  - Verified 2026-10-17 by running, in the owner's macOS repository before the Stage A locator commit (`HEAD` was then `2e496ca`; the B-120 files were new): `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_fresh_process_golden.py -v` → 2 passed; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **147 passed** in 0.49 s (the 2 new tests included; this total predates the 150 Stage A tests added later). Mutation check in scratch copies outside the repository: changing one figure in the golden (`1,740.00`→`1,740.01`) and changing the same figure in the PDF text each made the comparison test fail; the unmutated test passed again
  - Re-run after the Stage A commit `e0ce786` (2026-10-17, in the owner's macOS repository, run directly): `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_fresh_process_golden.py -v` → 2 passed in 0.30 s; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **297 passed** in 0.68 s. The working tree was `e0ce786` plus the B-120 test and golden file and other uncommitted documentation edits; the 297 is the 147 of the earlier run plus the 150 Stage A tests (as recorded in the Stage A entry), and it includes the two B-120 tests, which are added by the same commit as this entry. **Limitations:** tested on one machine only; the test does not itself assert that installed dependency versions match `uv.lock` (it relies on running under `uv run --locked`); the mutation checks above were not repeated. The tree made of `e0ce786` plus only this commit's files has not been tested independently: the 2 focused and 297 full-suite results are for the working tree as it was then
  - Limitations: one machine and one locked dependency set (`uv.lock`), so it does not show cross-machine determinism; a pdfplumber/pdfminer upgrade may legitimately change the golden and needs a reviewed regeneration; only reportlab's standard fonts are drawn (no `₹`, no real-report layouts); the child shares the venv interpreter. The 145-test figure from the proposal was not separately verified; the earlier run only reports the actual total of 147
  - PRD Section Q Phase 1 step 2 exit evidence ("test passes") is now recorded. **Phase 1 is not complete** (B-133 owner review, D-14a, the Stage A assignment-record work, and the other exit criteria are open)
- [ ] Commit 5 — D-01 table-extraction check: **synthetic half done 2026-10-13; public-report half: evidence collected 2026-10-13 (see the B-133 entry below), accepted by the owner with the provenance limitation and closed administratively as B-133 on 2026-10-18**, so D-01 (outcome deferred) and the Phase 1 exit criterion are **not yet met**
  - Method: synthetic statement pages drawn with reportlab (right-aligned figure columns, note column, two-line and FY-style headers, section headings, wrapped labels, blank cells, nil dashes, bracketed negatives, Indian grouping, a landscape page with two statements side by side), passed through the project's `PdfplumberExtractor`; tolerance limits measured by varying spacing and offsets one at a time. Scratch scripts were not committed
  - Works (synthetic): aligned rows stay on one line with columns in order; `(245.10)`, `-`, `–`, `1,23,456.70` and note references are kept verbatim; two-line headers keep dates in column order; section headings stay on their own line; page text stays per physical page, so citations by page number are unaffected
  - Fails (synthetic, default settings): (1) a blank cell leaves no placeholder; (2) a figure more than 3pt off its row's baseline moves to its own line; (3) figures 3pt or less apart merge into one token; (4) letter spacing above 3pt splits a figure into characters; (5) side-by-side statements share text lines. Cases (3) and (4) make FR-005.2 fail, so such values become `requires_review` (safe). Cases (1) and (2) are the dangerous ones for OD-1 option (a): in case (2) the row reads `Borrowings 14 98,765.40`, and matching by text order alone could attribute the comparative figure to the current period
  - OD-1 evidence (**OD-1 remains open**): pdfplumber word positions placed both figures of case (2) under the correct column headers by horizontal position, so option (b) handled the case that defeats option (a). Word-position extraction is **promising on synthetic evidence only**; validation on real public reports is required before OD-1 is decided
  - Not tested: the `₹` symbol (reportlab's built-in fonts cannot draw it; the synthetic page extracted it as `n`), real fonts, kerning, scanned-then-OCRed text layers, and tables drawn as images
  - Limitation of the sample: synthetic layouts are idealised; tolerance thresholds are pdfplumber defaults (3pt) and real reports may differ. No claim is made about real-document extraction quality
- [x] Public annual-report check (D-01, B-133) — **evidence collected 2026-10-13; findings accepted by the project owner with the provenance limitation on 2026-10-18; B-133 closed administratively by the owner on 2026-10-18 (see the closure bullet below); the D-01 outcome remains deferred (source URLs and retrieval dates unavailable). D-01 acceptance, OD-1 and the Phase 1 exit remain open.** Procedure: `docs/d01_public_report_checklist.md`; row-level text, renders, and logs are in git-ignored `private/d01/`
  - Documents (both **tuning set**, assigned 2026-10-13 before inspection; never to be used as held-out): R1 Infosys Integrated Annual Report 2024-25 (369 pages, Ind AS, ₹ crore, standalone and consolidated); R2 Microsoft FY2025 financial statements (80 pages, US GAAP, USD millions). Source URLs and retrieval dates are not recorded in this file (owner to supply and confirm; under PRD O.2 they belong in the git-ignored `private/d01/` record); no retrieval date is stated here
  - Pages inspected: R1 physical 194–197 (standalone) and 274–277 (consolidated); R2 physical 36 (income statements) and 38 (balance sheets). Expected values read from rendered pages
  - Ingestion: both OK with default settings; 46.4 s for R1 (369 pages), 16.0 s for R2 (80 pages); no PDF page labels in either; both start with `%PDF-`
  - Row results (34 rows classified; 2 fields not presented): **OK 28, SPLIT 4, LABEL-WRAP 2, MERGED 0, OFF-ROW 0, REORDERED 0, LOST 0**. All 4 SPLITs are in R2 (`88, 136`, `72, 361`, `268, 477`, `(4,901 )`); printed figures are intact on the rendered pages, so these are extraction splits. Under FR-005.2 they would become `requires_review` (safe, but unusable)
  - Faithfully extracted: `₹` (U+20B9), Indian grouping (`1,24,936`), Western grouping on R1's consolidated P&L as printed (`162,990`), brackets (`(963)`), nil `–`, decimal note references (`2.17`), and compound note references
  - Word positions (OD-1 evidence): every probed figure in both reports, and every SPLIT fragment, fell under the correct period header by horizontal position; decimal note references fell under "Note". **Caveat:** a naive nearest-column rule also assigned a number inside a wrapped label (R2 p38, `7,434`, far left of the figure columns) to the 2025 column, so a position-based method would need a maximum-distance rule. Promising, but two documents only — **OD-1 not decided**
  - Locator (OD-3 tuning evidence, no settings changed): R1 selected 6 of 8 statement pages, missed 197 and 277 (continuation pages with OCI/EPS only; no H.3 field), plus 3 false positives (p30 summary page; notes p263, p346). R2: **0 candidates** — plural titles ("BALANCE SHEETS", "INCOME STATEMENTS") fail the boundary rule and US line-item wording is not in the phrase lists

The skip is the check that the file only extends its HEAD version: it skips with a stated reason while the file is untracked, and runs once the file is committed. Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repository (compact form, move direction, date strictness, LF-only splitting, prefix check, SHA-256 uniqueness, CR, byte-order mark, same-SHA-256 and empty-reason checks, each disabled in turn) each made at least one test fail; these are not a systematic mutation-testing result
  - Limits (PRD O.2): the validator checks that the file is consistent within itself and, with git, that it only grows. It does not check a SHA-256 against a PDF (the PDFs are private), cannot prove that an assignment preceded inspection (commit order is the only evidence), and cannot stop a deliberate rewrite of both the file and git history. The `assigned_on` value 2026-10-13 is the date recorded in `PROGRESS.md`, not the commit date. A whitespace-only `reason` is accepted (only an empty or null reason fails). No held-out document is assigned; OQ-3 stays open

- [ ] Phase 2 step 2: schema (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; no live LLM call, no secret**): `src/credit_intel/models.py` holds the Pydantic schema. Enums for the H.3 field set (12 extractable fields, plus the derived-only `ebit`, which the model may never return), the four H.2 statuses, the H.1 reasons, scale, basis, period role, value interpretation and the H.1.2 attribute names. `make_field_id`. `ExtractedField` (H.1) with the conditional "Required" rules enforced as invariants, `Validation`, `Derivation`, `Evidence`, `Candidate` and `SourceDocument`. `DocumentAttribute` (H.1.2). The LLM response schema `ExtractionResponse` (`ProposedField`, `ProposedAttribute`): unknown names and attributes are rejected (FR-004.2), `raw_value` must be a string, and the generated JSON schema forbids additional properties. Row B-202; `tests/unit/test_schema.py` (91 tests). Pydantic is now used for the first time.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) H.1 asks for a `status_reason` whenever the status is not `extracted` but lists no reason for `not_found` or `conflicting`, so those two may omit it and only `requires_review` requires one; (2) the shape of the LLM response: per field `field_name`, `period_role`, `raw_value`, `scale`, `currency`, `page_number`, `evidence_quote`, `line_item_label`, with the period end and statement basis taken from the document-level attributes rather than returned per field; (3) `validation` holds the eight check results as true, false or null (null = not evaluated) plus a free `details` record for the column, anchor and derivation notes; (4) `period_end` is recorded once per role in `DocumentAttribute`; (5) the module is `credit_intel/models.py`, as in the PRD's source tree. Not decided here: which fields are required for FR-006.1 (step 8), and the reason code for an unparsable value (step 3; FR-004.3 gives none).

Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repository: 17 deliberate faults (extracted without all checks, no field_id check, a reason allowed on an extracted field, a value allowed when not extracted, floats accepted, conflicting without candidates, unknown attributes ignored, `ebit` extractable, currency and sha256 forms unchecked, source attributes not required, a nil value unchecked, requires_review without a reason, any period length, derived with source data, mutable records, period_end without a role) were each caught. Unchanged: Phase 2 offline only; OD-1 and OD-8 decided; D-01 outcome deferred
- [ ] Phase 2 step 3: value normalisation (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; no live LLM call, no secret**): new package `src/credit_intel/extraction/` with `normalise.py`. `parse_value(raw)` reads a raw printed value into a `Decimal` (FR-004.3, FR-004.4) and returns `None` when it cannot be read with certainty; `apply_scale(value, scale)` applies the declared scale exactly. Accepted: Western or Indian grouping (or none), a decimal part, enclosing brackets or one attached minus as a negative marker (reported), one leading currency symbol, and the nil convention (a lone `-`, `–`, `—` or `nil` is a reported zero, D-08). Rejected as unparsable: malformed grouping, a decimal comma, exponents, a plus sign, internal spaces (so a split figure like `(4,901 )` fails safe, matching the OD-1 decision), unbalanced or doubled markers, a detached minus, other currency forms (ISO codes, `Rs.`), non-ASCII digits, and strings over 64 characters. Row B-203; `tests/unit/test_value_normalisation.py` (122 tests).
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) the reason code for an unparsable value is the placeholder `period_not_verified` (`UNPARSABLE_REASON`), as the OD-1 proposal states; FR-004.3 names none (U-1, U-2); (2) the currency symbols accepted are `$ ₹ € £ ¥`; ISO codes and `Rs.` are not, and fail safe; (3) malformed thousands grouping is unparsable (the PRD's "grouping validity" rule, U-3, is not decided, and rejecting is the safe side); (4) a 64-character limit on the raw value, to bound untrusted input; (5) a zero in brackets reads as zero with the negative marker reported, and there is no negative zero. The sign check against the surrounding quote (FR-005.6) is step 7, not here.
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_value_normalisation.py -q` → 122 passed; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **614 passed** (492 plus 122). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repository: 15 deliberate faults (malformed grouping accepted, Indian grouping rejected, unbalanced or doubled markers accepted, the negative not applied, a case-sensitive nil, U+2212 read as nil, a float used, the length guard removed, a detached minus accepted, NFKC skipped, a wrong crore exponent, scientific-form scale output, the provisional reason changed) were caught; one mutant (a redundant zero guard) was equivalent, because a unary minus on zero stays zero, so the guard was simplified; and one mutation's anchor was mistyped, was rerun with the right anchor, and was caught. Unchanged: Phase 2 offline only; OD-1 and OD-8 decided; D-01 outcome deferred
- [ ] Phase 2 step 4: LLM provider layer (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; no live LLM call, no OpenAI provider yet, no secret, no `.env`**): new package `src/credit_intel/llm/`. `provider.py` holds the `LLMProvider` protocol (one blocking `complete(request) -> response with usage`), `LLMRequest`, `LLMResponse`, `LLMUsage`, `canonical_json`, and `FakeProvider` (scripted replies in order, plus recorded replies found by request fingerprint; a request with no reply raises rather than inventing one; every call is kept in `calls`). `cache.py` is the FR-003.5 response cache: `.cache/llm/<key>.json`, key = SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the inputs, entry = inputs, response text, usage and UTC creation time, a hit only when the stored inputs equal the current ones, atomic write (temporary file then `os.replace`), hit and miss counts. `budget.py` holds the OD-8 check (estimate = UTF-8 bytes / 2 rounded up; used + estimate + reserved maximum output over the ceiling raises `E-LLM-BUDGET-EXCEEDED`; the provider's reported usage is what is recorded). `gateway.py` joins them: a hit makes no call and counts zero; a miss checks the budget, calls, records usage and writes the entry. `config.py` gains the required `max_output_tokens` setting (env `CREDIT_INTEL_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`; same rules as the ceiling; shipped as 4096), `E-LLM-BUDGET-EXCEEDED` is added to the error codes, and `.cache/` to `.gitignore`.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) `max_output_tokens` is a required setting, shipped as 4096 (the ceiling stays unset); (2) temperature is fixed at 0 and the structured-output mode at `json_schema`, constants rather than settings, both in the cache key; (3) the fake provider supports both scripted and recorded replies; (4) the estimate of two UTF-8 bytes per token; (5) the cache key carries an extra `corrective_feedback_sha256` input (null for a first attempt), which FR-003.5's list does not name: without it a FR-004.1 corrective retry, sent for the same pages, would collide with the first call's key and replay the invalid response. **This is a small addition to the specified key list and needs an owner decision on whether to amend FR-003.5.** (6) Open for step 5 and later: a response is cached when received, before it is validated, so an invalid response would be replayed on a re-run; the caller will need to decide whether to cache only validated responses. Not built: the OpenAI provider, retries with backoff (FR-011.3), and the manifest fields.
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_llm_layer.py -q` (49 tests) and the settings tests (39 plus 13 new, with existing inline settings updated for the new required setting); `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **676 passed** (614 plus 49 new tests here and 13 in the settings tests). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repo: 13 mutants (budget comparison, reservation, rounding, bytes-per-token, cache input comparison, atomic write, key sorting, usage recording, cache write, budget check, recorded replies, hit count, environment override), all caught.
- [ ] Phase 2 step 5: versioned prompt and extraction flow (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; no live LLM call, no secret, fake provider only**): `prompts/extraction_v1.md` (version header `prompt-version: extraction-v1`; the response JSON schema and the field list are filled in from the code so the prompt cannot drift from the schema), `extraction/prompt.py` (loader; `PromptError` on a malformed file), and `extraction/extract.py`: `extract_fields(document, located, gateway, llm, prompt, basis)`. Gates before any call, in order: no candidate pages gives `E-EXTRACT-NO-STATEMENT-PAGES`; then the configured basis absent gives `E-EXTRACT-BASIS-UNAVAILABLE` (existing `check_statement_basis`). Pages are sent in ascending order, each between `=== PAGE n ===` lines. Output is parsed as strict JSON and validated against `ExtractionResponse`; invalid output gets one corrective retry whose message includes a description built only from error locations and types (never values), whose cache key carries that description's SHA-256, and whose tokens count; a second failure raises `InvalidOutputError` (`E-LLM-INVALID-OUTPUT`) with the last raw response attached. `LLMGateway.complete` takes an `is_valid` callback and caches only valid responses (PRD v0.4.19). New codes: `E-EXTRACT-NO-STATEMENT-PAGES`, `E-LLM-INVALID-OUTPUT`.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) the cache stores only validated responses (default proposed; PRD v0.4.19, above); (2) the raw invalid response is carried on the exception (`raw_response`), and writing it to a run directory is deferred to the pipeline glue (step 9); (3) the prompt wording, for owner review in `prompts/extraction_v1.md`; (4) the basis gate also applies when a page range was supplied, as the PRD text does not exempt it, so a supplied range with no recognisable title of the configured basis stops the run; (5) the prompt text is not part of the cache key beyond its version string, so a prompt edit must bump the version; (6) the JSON-schema structured-output mode is named in the key but the provider-specific schema is sent only when the OpenAI provider exists (step 10); (7) the schema version is the constant `1` in `extract.py`; (8) the retry message appends the rejection to the original pages and does not include the previous response; (9) transient-failure retries with backoff (FR-011.3) are not built.
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_extraction_flow.py -q` (41 tests) and `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **718 passed** (676 plus 41 here and 1 in the LLM layer tests). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repo: 13 mutants (gate removed, basis check removed, page order, retry sharing the first key, caching invalid responses in the flow and in the gateway, attempts count, raw response lost, feedback uncapped, feedback leaking input values, no feedback in the retry, placeholder check, page tags), all caught after one test was strengthened (page order was first an equivalent-looking survivor on a two-page input).
- [ ] Phase 2 step 6 plan (2026-10-19; saved at the owner's request; **a plan, not an approval of later sub-steps**): `WordExtractor` and the OD-1 period-column verifier (AC-OD1-1 to AC-OD1-26). It returns a per-field column verdict; statuses are decided in step 7 in the FR-005.3 order. FR-005.9 validated period-ends (a map from anchor to period-end) and the FR-005.10 row-label result (pass or fail per field) are injected inputs, because those checks are built in step 7; anchor *form* recognition is in step 6. Sub-steps, each built unstaged and committed on the owner's instruction: **6a** word extraction (`Word`, `PageWords`, the separate `WordExtractor` protocol, `PdfplumberExtractor.extract_words`, `ColumnSettings`; AC-OD1-24 golden words in a fresh process, AC-OD1-25); **6b** structure (lines, header block, anchors, regions, the note span, structure validity S, the no-usable-anchor and rotation fail-safes; AC-OD1-1 to 5, 10, 21, 22, 23, ZA-1); **6c** assignment (quote-to-line linkage, label limit, conditions B, R, N, U, the outcomes table, number forms, blank cells, a record of every condition's result; AC-OD1-6 to 9, 11 to 20, NC-1, NT-1); **6d** properties and the final mutation sweep (AC-OD1-21 matrix, 24, 26). Defaults taken (owner: "yes, save the plan and start 6a", after the recommendation to take all five): (1) `extract_words` rejects an empty, duplicate or out-of-range page list with `E-INPUT-PAGE-RANGE`, accepts any order and returns ascending; (2) pdfplumber `x_tolerance` and `y_tolerance` pinned at 3; (3) `column_margin_pt` = 3 and `line_tolerance_pt` = 3 are dataclass defaults, not in `settings.toml`, and stay provisional; (4) the result records every condition with the column, anchor quote and token and anchor boxes; (5) synthetic data only, `private/` untouched. Still deferred, as decided: U-1 to U-4, U-7, U-8, the H1 guard, joined wrapped labels.
- [ ] Phase 2 step 6a: word extraction (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic PDFs only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `Word` and `PageWords` in `ingestion/models.py`; the separate `WordExtractor` protocol in `ingestion/extractor.py` (`TextExtractor` unchanged); `PdfplumberExtractor.extract_words(path, page_numbers)`, with `x_tolerance` and `y_tolerance` pinned at 3 and `keep_blank_chars=False`; words ordered by `(top, x0, text)`; width and height are the unrotated page size and rotation is `page.rotation % 360` (§15). Golden output for a fresh process: `tests/fixtures/word_extraction_golden.json` (generated by the test's own child script and reviewed: page 1's words sit at the placed x positions, the blank page 2 has no words). The golden holds exact floats from this machine's pdfplumber 0.11.10; like B-120 it is regenerated only in a reviewed change. **AC-OD1-25 (word-line text equals the `extract_text` line) moves to 6b**, because it needs the 6b line-grouping function; the 6a plan table listed it here.

- [ ] Phase 2 step 6b: page structure (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic data only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `src/credit_intel/extraction/columns.py`. `group_lines` (3.2: sort by `(cy, x0, text)`, a new line when the centre exceeds the previous word's by more than `line_tolerance_pt`, NFKC, empty words dropped); `header_block` (below the last title line and above the first figure line, using the locator's `title_lines` and `is_figure_word` unchanged); `find_anchors` (a four-digit year; `FY2025`, `2024-25`, `FY2024-25`, an `FY` word plus an adjacent year form; ISO and numeric dates as one word; written dates of three adjacent words in either order; the date words' union extent is the anchor, and the "as at" words are not part of it); `analyse_structure(page, resolve_period, ...)` returning a `PageStructure` with the lines, header-block bounds, anchors (each with its resolved period-end), columns, the note span, the label limit and the problems (`rotated`, `no_usable_anchor`, `unvalidated_anchor`, `overlapping_regions`, `duplicate_period_column`); `valid` is true when there are none. `resolve_period` is the injected FR-005.9 result, called once for each header-block anchor and never on a rotated page. Anchors of one period whose regions overlap (stacked headers) merge into one column; two non-overlapping columns for one period, or overlapping columns of different periods, are problems. The note span runs from the leftmost "Note" or "Notes" word's left edge minus the margin to the first region's left edge (exclusive), and is absent if there is no word or no room. No label limit is computed without a usable anchor. Covers AC-OD1-1 to 5, 10 (a to d), 21 (structure level), 22, 23, ZA-1 and AC-OD1-25.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) the adjacency test (P-3, provisional) uses the mean of the two words' average character widths, and overlapping words count as adjacent; (2) a four-digit year is any four digits (not only 19xx or 20xx), since an unvalidated anchor already fails safe; (3) the "as at" and "for the year ended" words are not part of an anchor; (4) an `FY` word joins only an adjacent year or `yyyy-yy` form; (5) a "Note" word at or right of the first period region defines no span; (6) the recognition of anchor *forms* lives here, while date parsing and validation (FR-005.9) are step 7, so the two must agree on the accepted forms (step 7 should test that every form recognised here can be parsed there); (7) title lines are the located-statement title lines only, not the other-statement titles of rule 3c; (8) every problem is reported, not only the first, and the reason code chosen later is still `period_not_verified` (with FR-005.3's order giving `period_mismatch` for an unvalidated period). Not built: quote linkage and the assignment conditions (6c).
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_column_structure.py -q` → 85 passed; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **835 passed** (750 plus 85). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repo: 28 mutants (line tolerance, chaining, word order, sorting, empty words, NFKC, title end, figure line, adjacency comparison and mean, overlap rule, rotation, each problem, margin, note span, FY forms, anchor extent, header block, column order, stacked anchors); all caught after two tests were added (the adjacency mean was not distinguished from the larger width because all test words had equal character widths; the absolute-gap mutant needed overlapping words). One mutant was equivalent: restricting the overlap check to different periods is redundant, because columns of one period never overlap once merged, so the condition was removed and a comment added.
- [ ] Phase 2 step 6c: assignment (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic data only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `src/credit_intel/extraction/assignment.py`. `check_column(structure, claim)` takes a `ColumnClaim(period_end, raw_value, evidence_quote, label_ok)` and returns a `ColumnCheck` with `outcome` (pass or fail), the first `failure` in evaluation order, `reasons` (FR-005.3 order: `label_not_recognised` first, then `period_not_verified`), a `Conditions` record (S structure, linked, column, B boundary, U unique, N number, value match, R row label; `None` for a condition not reached), and, for traceability, the figure line, the column and its anchors, the words in the region and, only on a pass, the assigned token and its parsed value. Evaluation order: S; link the quote to one word-line (3.5: the raw value on exactly one quote line, and exactly one page line containing that quote line); the claimed period has a column; B (every numeric or nil token right of the label limit overlaps exactly one period region, or none but touches the note span; corrected in step 6d, below); U (the claimed region holds exactly one value-like word and nothing else except a separate currency symbol); N (a numeric or nil token that `parse_value` accepts); the value match (absolute value and interpretation equal, so brackets and minus are left to FR-005.6, and a nil dash does not match a proposed zero). R is the caller's FR-005.10 result; a failed R leaves no assignment. Covers AC-OD1-6 to 9, 11 to 20, NC-1 and NT-1 (AC-OD1-21 to 23 are in 6b). `CURRENCY_SYMBOLS` is now public in `normalise.py` and `clean_text` in `columns.py`.

  - **Code correction found by the end-to-end test (owner decision, 2026-10-19: option B).** Step 6c counted the note span as a region in condition B, so a right-aligned figure wider than its header, reaching into the note span, overlapped two regions and unverified its whole line; in the realistic layout most rows failed. FR-005.7 says a word is the value of a column only if its extent overlaps "that column's region and no other", and it calls the note area a "span", not a region, so the PRD text already supports the owner's choice: B counts **period regions only**. A token that overlaps exactly one period region belongs to it even if its left end lies in the note span; one that overlaps no period region is a note reference if it touches the note span and a stray token otherwise; one that overlaps two period regions is unverified. The 6c unit test that expected the old behaviour was rewritten, and tests were added for a wide figure reaching into the note span (with and without a note reference beside it), a token crossing the note span and two period regions, and a note reference partly in the span. The generator now places figures up to 30 pt left of their header. No PRD change was needed; an explanatory sentence in FR-005.7 can be added as v0.4.20 if the owner wants one (not applied). The unapproved OD-1 proposal note (§3.6, §3.7) still reads ambiguously and is left as it is.

- [ ] Phase 2 step 7a: period-end validation (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic strings only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `src/credit_intel/extraction/periods.py`. `parse_full_date` (a written date in either order, an ISO date, or an unambiguous numeric date, with the optional prefixes "as at", "as of", "year ended", "for the year ended"; an impossible date, an ambiguous numeric date such as `03/04/2025`, a zero day or month, mixed separators and trailing text are never read); `parse_year_token` (`2025`, `FY2025`, `2024-25`, `FY 2024-25` to the later year; the two-digit end must be the next year modulo 100); `classify` (full date, ambiguous numeric date, invalid date, year token, bad financial year, unrecognised); `find_full_dates` (every accepted full date anywhere in the page text, not glued to other digits); `resolve_anchor_texts` (a full date validates itself; a year token is validated only by the one full date of that year on the page, none or two different ones is not guessed; then a year token exactly one year before the current period, the latest validated period-end, is validated as the comparative with the same month and day, unless the current period ends on 28 or 29 February; each result carries the raw text, the ISO date, the method and, for a derived comparative, the current period-end it came from, or the reason it was not validated); `periods_are_consecutive` (H.1.1, with 28 and 29 February as the same day); `page_period_resolver` (the `resolve_period` callable for `analyse_structure`, using the same header-block anchors; a rotated page resolves nothing). Covers Appendix B.6 rows 3 to 6 and 22 and the H.1.1 boundary cases.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) "a full date elsewhere on the same statement page" is read as any accepted full date anywhere in the page's text, and it validates a year token only when exactly one distinct date has that year (two different dates in one year are not guessed, for example a period start and end); (2) "the later validated date is the current period" is taken as the latest validated period-end, so a comparative-only page does not make the current year valid; (3) numeric dates with a zero day or month are invalid rather than ambiguous; (4) a year token is validated by a full date even if the anchor and the date are on different lines; (5) the accepted forms are exactly those the 6b anchor finder recognises (a test checks every 6b form is classified here), so the comma after a day in either order, an optional full stop after a month, and ordinal suffixes are accepted; (6) role assignment (current or comparative) and the consecutive-period check on a pair are document-level and belong to 7d, which will use `periods_are_consecutive`; (7) with three period columns, which period is the comparative stays unresolved (U-4).

Four mutants were equivalent and were not chased: a month alternation order (regex backtracking), a redundant comparison in two numeric-date branches (the date constructor rejects the same cases), and a redundant form test, which was simplified.

  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) FR-005.10 cuts the label at the first figure *before* markers are removed, so a marker that is itself a numeric token (`12`, `1)`, `(1)`, a spaced `( i )`) ends the label and the row fails safe (`label_not_recognised`); markers such as `I.`, `(a)`, `iv)`, `a.`, `1.` and bare Roman numerals or letters work; (2) a nil dash or the word nil counts as a figure for the cut, so a dash inside a label ("Profit - after tax") shortens it and fails safe; (3) the label is taken from the page's figure line, so step 7e will compute it after linking the quote, and an unlinkable quote leaves the label unevaluated rather than failing it; (4) a section heading is a line whose normalised text equals a configured heading (a marker and a trailing colon allowed), scanning upward past item rows, and a line starting with "total" and naming liabilities ends the section, so a continued page with no heading fails safe; (5) a quote from a page that is not a candidate page is `evidence_not_verified`; (6) the raw value is searched for as a substring of the quote, as FR-005.2 is written, and the column check is what ties it to one cell.

- [ ] Phase 2 step 7c: sign, scale and currency (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic text only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `extraction/runs.py` (`statement_run_start(result, page, type)`: FR-013 rule 6, consecutive selected pages sharing a statement type; none for a page not selected for that type, which includes a page range), and `extraction/declarations.py`. `check_sign(raw, token)` (FR-005.6): the occurrence on the page (the word the column check assigned, which carries its brackets or attached minus) and the parsed raw value must agree; a reported nil passes. `find_scales` and `check_scale` (FR-005.8): a scale is declared by "in" plus a scale word with an optional currency token between ("₹ in crore", "(Rs. in lakh)", "in millions") or a thousands mark (`'000`, `(000s)`, `in 000s`); the claimed scale must be the one the cited page declares; two different scales on the cited page are `scale_ambiguous`; if the cited page declares none, the first page of its statement run is used the same way; otherwise `scale_not_verified`. `check_currency`: an uppercase whole-word ISO code, a symbol or a name on the cited page, else on the first page of the run. `config.py` gains `DeclarationSettings` (provisional phrase lists built from the PRD's examples) and `FIELD_STATEMENT_TYPES`. Covers Appendix B Examples 2 and 3 and B.6 rows 1, 11 to 13 (the exact scaling is `apply_scale`, step 3).
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) the sign marker is taken from the occurrence on the page (the assigned token), not from the raw value alone, so a bracketed raw value for an unbracketed cell is a mismatch; FR-005.2 would usually reject it first; (2) the cited page's own scale declaration takes precedence over the first page of the run (FR-005.8 says "or"; a lakh claim is not accepted on a crore page just because the first page says lakh); (3) two scales on the first page of the run are ambiguous when the cited page declares none; (4) a field's statement type, needed to find its run, is a table in `config.py` (`FIELD_STATEMENT_TYPES`) because the PRD does not tabulate it; (5) a scale of `units` needs a declaration ("in rupees", "in actuals", "in units"), as decided; (6) a scale word is matched only after "in", so "a guarantee of 5 crore" is not a declaration but prose such as "growth in crores of rupees" is; (7) an ISO code is matched in capitals only, so "ALL" or "TRY" in ordinary text is not a currency; `$` supports USD only and `¥` JPY only; (8) a page that declares a second currency does not make the claimed one unsupported, because FR-005.8 specifies only `currency_not_verified` for no support.

- [ ] Phase 2 step 7d: document-level attributes (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic text only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `extraction/attributes.py`. `verify_attributes(proposed, pages)` checks the five H.1.2 attributes and returns `VerifiedAttributes` (six `DocumentAttribute` records, the period evidence, and `period_end(role)`). Every attribute needs its quote on the cited candidate page and its raw text in the quote (FR-005.1, .2). `period_end`: the raw text must be in the page's title or column header (above its first figure line), validate under FR-005.9 (step 7a, with the current period passed on so a comparative year on another page can still be derived), and its ISO date must equal the normalised value; the later validated date must be the current period; the comparative must pass the consecutive-period rule (H.1.1); any failure is `period_mismatch`. `statement_basis`: the raw text must be in a title block (H.4.6, the locator's new public `title_block_bases`) that names exactly the proposed basis (`consolidated` or `standalone`); `unknown`, a made-up value, no title block and a basis word outside a block are all `basis_unknown`. `reporting_currency`: an ISO code that the quote itself supports (`currency_not_verified`). `scale`: a known scale that the quote itself declares (`scale_not_verified`, or `scale_ambiguous` for two). Not proposed is `not_found`. Several proposals of one attribute are one record when the verified ones agree, are not guessed between when they disagree, and report the first failure when none verifies. `blocking_reason(attributes, role)` is the dependency rule: a field is blocked by its own period (`period_mismatch`), then the basis (`basis_unknown`), currency (`currency_not_verified`) and scale (`scale_not_verified` or `scale_ambiguous`); the entity name blocks nothing. The locator gains `title_block_bases` (and `check_statement_basis` now uses it), and `resolve_anchor_texts` accepts a `known_current`. Covers B.6 rows 3 to 6 and 22 and the attribute side of row 9.

- [ ] Phase 2 step 7e: field verification and assembly (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic PDFs only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `extraction/verify.py`. `VerificationContext` holds the source document, the candidate pages' text and words, the locator result, the verified attributes and the settings; `FieldVerifier.verify(proposed)` (and `verify_all(response)`) runs every deterministic check on one proposed field and builds its `ExtractedField`: presence (7b), the figure line (6c's now public `link_quote`) and the row label on it (7b), the period column for the attribute-validated period (6), the sign on the assigned token (7c), the scale and currency on the cited page or the first page of its statement run (7c), and the attribute blockers (7d, now `blocking_reasons`, with `blocking_reason` as its first). A field is `extracted` only if nothing failed; otherwise `requires_review` with the first reason in the FR-005.3 order: `evidence_not_verified`, `value_not_in_evidence`, `label_not_recognised`, `period_mismatch`, `basis_unknown`, `period_not_verified`, `sign_mismatch`, `scale_not_verified` or `scale_ambiguous`, `currency_not_verified`. `FieldVerification.reasons` lists every failing reason in that order. The value is the parsed raw value, signed, scaled exactly in `Decimal` (a nil is 0); the currency is kept only when extracted; the quote, raw value and label are stored exactly as proposed; the field ID uses the attribute's validated period-end and basis (`unknown` if the basis is not verified). `Validation` records the eight results (`None` for a check not reached) and `details` holds the period evidence and derivation, the label match and section, the column conditions, region, anchor quotes, region words and the token's box, the sign, the scale and currency sources and the attribute blockers, all JSON-serialisable. `page_period_resolver` accepts the document's validated current period so a comparative-only page can derive its column. Covers Appendix B Examples 1 to 3 and B.6 rows 1, 2 and 11 to 14.

- [ ] Phase 2 step 8 plan (2026-10-19; saved at the owner's request; **a plan, not an approval of later sub-steps**): the final per-period field set (FR-006, H.4, FR-006.5). Derived values (EBIT, `BORR-SUM`) are Phase 3 (H.5, FR-007) and OD-9 stays open. **A defect in step 7e must be fixed first:** 7e took every field's statement basis from the one document-level `statement_basis` attribute, but H.4.6 reads a statement's basis from the title block of its own page, so a document with both consolidated and standalone statements would label every field alike and the "ignore other bases" rule could not work. Sub-steps, each built unstaged and committed on the owner's instruction: **8a** basis per field (H.4.6: from the cited page's title block, or the first page of its statement run for a continuation page; no block or both bases is `unknown` and `basis_unknown`; after verification fields of other bases than the configured basis are set aside and the bases present are recorded; B.6 rows 9 and 10); **8b** missing (FR-006.1, FR-004.3: a `not_found` record for every H.3 field and period without a verified record, never zero; needs a small schema change, below; B.6 row 14); **8c** reconciliation (H.4.1, H.4.2, H.4.6: group by field ID; the same value is one field; different pages are consistent under the precise rule (step sizes in base units, exact equality at equal precision, otherwise half a unit of the coarser value's last digit); the more precise value is used and the others become `corroborating_evidence`; beyond tolerance is `conflicting` with every candidate and its evidence; B.6 rows 7 and 8); **8d** plausibility and the result (FR-006.5: after reconciliation a negative `revenue`, `current_assets`, `current_liabilities`, `total_borrowings`, `borrowings_non_current`, `borrowings_current` or `interest_expense` is `requires_review` with `implausible_value`; negative `net_profit`, `profit_before_tax`, `operating_profit` and `total_equity` stay valid; one deterministic `FieldSet` for the run, property tests, a final mutation sweep). Defaults taken (owner: "yes, save the plan and start 8a", after the recommendation to take all eight): (1) a `not_found` record for all 12 H.3 fields in both periods, the required-or-optional distinction left to Phase 3 where metrics depend on it; (2) a `not_found` source record carries no evidence attributes, which relaxes the step 2 invariant, and H.1 gets a clarifying sentence that could ride with the optional FR-005.7 sentence as PRD v0.4.20 (not applied); (3) only `extracted` occurrences are reconciled, and unverified ones are listed in the record's details; (4) when two pages agree within tolerance the more precise value is used (H.4.2) and the primary statement page (the highest-scoring candidate page of the field's statement type, lowest page number on ties) breaks exact ties; (5) the document-level basis attribute stays as the check of the run's basis and each field also gets its page's basis; (6) plausibility applies after reconciliation to the final value, negative meaning below zero, and a reported nil is plausible; (7) `conflicting` keeps a null `status_reason`; (8) the unparsable-value reason stays the placeholder `period_not_verified`.
- [ ] Phase 2 step 8a: basis per field (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic data only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `extraction/bases.py`. `page_basis(text)` is the basis a page's own title blocks give (one basis, or `unknown` for none or several; `None` for a page with no title line); `field_basis(field, page, pages, located)` is the basis of the statement a field was read from (the cited page's own title, else the first page of its statement run for the field's statement type, else `unknown`, and where it was read); `filter_by_basis(items, configured, basis_of)` keeps the configured basis and the items with no basis to compare, sets other known bases aside, and records the bases present (D-06). `verify.py` now gives each field the basis of its own statement for the field ID (correcting the step 7e defect above) and adds `basis_unknown` when that basis is unknown; the document-level basis attribute still blocks every field when it is not extracted, and the field's `details` record the basis and where it was read. Covers B.6 rows 9 and 10 at the field level.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) a page with several title blocks that do not all name the same single basis is `unknown`; (2) a titled page of `unknown` basis does not borrow the basis of its run's first page (only an untitled page does); (3) a page range has no runs, so an untitled page there is `unknown`; (4) fields of `unknown` basis and fields with no basis are kept by the filter, because they may belong to the configured basis and are already `requires_review`; (5) the filter takes any items and a basis function, so 8c and 8d can use it on verifications or on final fields; (6) a statement with no basis wording is `unknown`, not standalone (OD-10 is unchanged).
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_statement_bases.py -q` → 20 passed and the changed `tests/integration/test_field_verification.py`; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **1411 passed** (1385 plus 20 here and 6 new field-verification tests; the one test that asserted the old `.unknown` ID was rewritten). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repo: 16 mutants (no title, mixed bases, a missing page, the page's own basis first, the run start, the statement type, the bases present, keeping unknown, the filter inverted, the verify wiring of `basis_unknown` and of the basis, and the locator settings on both pages); all caught after four tests were added (a page without basis wording beside a verified document basis, the locator settings on the cited page and on the run's first page, and the mixed-basis case). One mutant was equivalent: excluding an `unknown` single block name is redundant, because the basis `unknown` maps to the same value, so the condition was simplified.
- [ ] Phase 2 step 8b: missing fields (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic data only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `extraction/missing.py`. `missing_fields(present, attributes, basis, source_document)` returns a `not_found` record for each of the twelve H.3 fields (never `ebit`) and each period whose end date was validated, that has no record in `present`, in field order with the current period first, with the run's basis in the ID and no value, currency, reason or evidence (never zero; a reported nil is a different record, a dash the document printed). A field that was proposed and failed (`requires_review`), is `conflicting`, or has an unknown basis already has a record, so nothing is added beside it. `models.py` (the step 2 schema): a `not_found` source field now needs only its `source_document`, because it has no evidence to carry; every other source status still needs all the evidence attributes. Covers B.6 row 14 and FR-006.1, FR-004.3.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) all twelve H.3 fields are treated as expected in both periods, and the required-or-optional distinction is left to Phase 3 where metrics depend on it (FR-006.1); (2) a period whose end date was not validated gets no `not_found` records (its absence is reported by the period-end attribute, and a record needs a date for its ID); (3) the `not_found` record keeps `status_reason` null, as the schema already allowed; (4) **schema interpretation:** H.1 says the source attributes are "required when origin = source", which cannot hold for a field that was not found; the relaxation above is my reading, and a clarifying sentence in H.1 ("for a `not_found` source field, only `source_document`") could be added with the optional FR-005.7 sentence as PRD v0.4.20 (not applied); (5) the run's configured basis is the basis of the new records, and a record with the configured basis, another field's unknown basis, or any status counts as present.
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_missing_fields.py -q` → 16 passed and 3 new schema tests; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **1430 passed** (1411 plus 16 here and 3 in the schema tests). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repo: 11 mutants (EBIT included, a missing date, an existing record ignored, only extracted counted, the wrong key, the status, the basis, a zero value, the order, and both schema conditions); all caught.
- [ ] Phase 2 step 8c: reconciliation (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic data only, `private/` untouched, no live call**): `extraction/reconcile.py`. `reconcile(fields, located)` returns one record per field ID, in field order (current period first, then basis), from every occurrence of it. Only `extracted` occurrences are reconciled: identical values agree; values printed with the same precision must be exactly equal; with different precision they agree if they differ by at most half a unit of the coarser value's last printed digit in base units, `|a - b| <= 0.5 * 10**-p * s` (H.4.2), where the printed places are read from the raw value (so `1,234.50` has two) and `s` is the scale size; when they agree the most precise value is used (a tie goes to the primary statement page, then the lowest page number) and the others become `corroborating_evidence`, without repeating identical evidence; otherwise the field is `conflicting` with no value or currency, a null reason, every distinct candidate with its value and evidence, and the rule, values and primary page in `validation.details`. Occurrences that failed verification are never merged: beside a verified one they are listed in `validation.details.unverified_occurrences`; with none verified the best-ranked failed one is the record and the rest are in `other_occurrences`. `primary_page` is the highest-scoring candidate page of the field's statement type (lowest page number on a tie). Records are rebuilt through the schema, so every result is validated. Covers B.6 rows 7 and 8 and FR-006.2.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) the most precise value is the reference and every other verified occurrence must agree with it (not pairwise), so three occurrences are not chained through intermediates; (2) a conflicting record's own evidence attributes are the primary page's (the schema requires them); (3) the same page, quote and raw value proposed twice is one occurrence, not corroboration; (4) a reported nil has zero places and agrees with a zero, not with a figure; (5) negative and positive figures never agree; (6) `corroborating_evidence` keeps page, label, quote, line label and raw value but not a value (the `Evidence` model has none), so the corroborating value is its raw value; (7) ranking uses the page, raw value and quote, not the input order, so the result does not depend on the order of the occurrences.
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_reconciliation.py -q` → 34 passed (including a fixed-seed property test over 300 generated occurrence sets); `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **1464 passed** (1430 plus 34). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repo: 20 mutants (the half-unit comparison and its inclusion, the coarse step, which is finer, the printed places, the scale size, the most precise reference, tie ranking, the primary page, `all` against `any`, candidate and corroborating de-duplication, the unverified listing, the failed choice, the primary score and tie, the output order); all caught after one test was added (the primary page wins an exact tie against a lower-numbered page). Two were equivalent: exact equality at equal precision is the same as the half-unit test, because printed values are whole multiples of their step, and an explicit null reason on a conflict (the occurrence was already extracted), which was removed.
