Covers FR-006.5, FR-006.1 to .3 at the record level, and B.6 rows 7, 8, 10 and 14 end to end.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) plausibility is applied after reconciliation to the final value (default 6), so a negative that disagrees with a positive is a conflict, not an implausible value; (2) the order is basis filter, reconcile, plausibility, missing; (3) `get` returns only the configured basis, so a record of unknown basis is in `fields` (the analyst sees it) but not returned by `get`; (4) `unplaced` verifications are reported and have no record; (5) the configured basis must be `consolidated` or `standalone`; (6) FR-006.3 (exclusion from calculations) is carried by the statuses themselves: Phase 3 reads only `extracted` fields, and FR-006.4 (the memo section) is a later phase.
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_plausibility.py tests/unit/test_field_set.py -q` → 55 and 22 passed, plus one integration test; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **1542 passed** (1464 plus 55, 22 and 1). Final mutation sweep for step 8 on scratch copies outside the repo, run against the whole suite: 18 mutants (each plausibility rule and the zero boundary, the reason, the nulled value; each stage of the pipeline and its order; the configured-basis check, unplaced, `get`, the output order, ignored, bases present); all caught after one test was added (`get` must not return a record of unknown basis). One mutant was equivalent: the status test in `is_implausible`, because only an extracted field has a value; the redundant check was removed.
- [ ] Phase 2 step 9 plan (2026-10-19; saved at the owner's request; **a plan, not an approval of later sub-steps**): the minimal offline glue from a PDF to the `FieldSet`. Section Q puts the run directory, manifest, `results.json`, the error catalogue with exit codes and the CLI (FR-009 to FR-012, FR-014, G.1) in Phase 5, and the Phase 2 exit criteria do not ask for them, so step 9 stops at the field set. `run_extraction(path, provider, llm, ...)` chains: ingest (FR-001, FR-002), locate (FR-013, or the supplied page range), extract (step 5: gates, one gateway call with cache and token ceiling, one corrective retry), word positions for the candidate pages only, attribute verification, field verification, and `build_field_set`. It returns an `ExtractionRun`: the field set, attributes, located pages, extraction result (prompt version, attempts), the source document and page count, run statistics (LLM calls, tokens used, cache hits and misses) and a flag when the model's basis attribute differs from the configured basis. Catalogued errors propagate as `IngestionError`, without exit codes. Sub-steps, each built unstaged and committed on the owner's instruction: **9a** the pipeline, the result and statistics, and stage logging with the standard `logging` module (FR-011 state names, stage names, counts and durations at INFO, no document text, quotes or values per FR-012.3); **9b** end-to-end scenarios with a scripted fake provider on reportlab PDFs (a clean statement; the wrong column, sign and scale mistakes; invalid output with the corrective retry and `E-LLM-INVALID-OUTPUT`; the token ceiling; no candidate pages; a missing configured basis; a page range), a re-run served from the cache with identical results, a fresh-process golden of the serialised field set (the determinism part of FR-012.2), and a final mutation sweep. Defaults taken (owner: "save the plan to PROGRESS.md unstaged and start 9a", after the recommendation to take all five): (1) stop at the `FieldSet`; the run directory, manifest, `results.json`, exit codes and CLI stay in Phase 5, and `InvalidOutputError.raw_response` stays on the exception until then; (2) the configured basis is a function parameter defaulting to `consolidated`, and the `settings.toml` entry and `--basis` flag arrive with FR-014; (3) a model basis attribute that differs from the configured basis does not block anything, since each field carries its own page's basis (8a), and is recorded in the run result; (4) the pipeline takes a provider object, and the OpenAI provider, transient-failure retries with backoff (FR-011.3) and the live run belong to step 10; (5) INFO logs carry stage names, counts and durations only.
- [ ] Phase 2 step 9a: the offline pipeline (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic PDFs and a scripted fake provider only, `private/` untouched, no live call, no network**): `extraction/pipeline.py`. `run_extraction(path, provider, llm, *, basis=consolidated, page_range=None, cache_dir=.cache/llm, prompt=None, extractor=None, <settings>)` ingests and validates the PDF, locates the statement pages (or uses the supplied range), checks that the configured basis is present, makes the one extraction call through the cache and token ceiling (step 5), reads word positions for the candidate pages only, verifies the attributes and every proposed field, and builds the field set. It returns an `ExtractionRun`: the source document and page count, the configured basis, the located pages, the extraction result (prompt version, attempts), the attributes, every `FieldVerification`, the `FieldSet`, `RunStats` (provider calls, tokens used, cache hits and misses) and `attribute_basis_differs`. Catalogued failures propagate as `IngestionError`. Standard-library logging at INFO writes one line per FR-011 stage (`received`, `validated`, `text_extracted`, `statements_located`, `fields_extracted`, `validated_fields`) with counts, milliseconds and the first 12 characters of the document hash, and `stage=failed after=<stage> code=<code>` on a catalogued failure; no document text, quote, file name, path or value is logged. A missing configured basis is reported after `statements_located` and no candidate pages after `fields_extracted`, as the G.1 catalogue lists them. `LLMGateway.calls` counts provider calls. Covers the Phase 2 offline end-to-end requirement for the clean path, the cache re-run and the failure paths; 9b adds the scenario suite.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) the basis gate now also runs in the `statements_located` stage so the failure is logged against the stage G.1 names, and again (harmlessly) inside `extract_fields`; (2) logs carry a 12-character document hash and no file name, since FR-012.3 allows identifiers but a file name can be sensitive; (3) a quote cited from a page that was not a candidate page is `evidence_not_verified`, and the field is reported with the `unknown` basis because a page that is not a statement has none, so it appears in the field set but not through `get`; (4) `attribute_basis_differs` is true only when the model's basis attribute was verified as extracted and names another basis; (5) the provider-call counter counts calls that returned, so a provider that raises is not counted (the real provider and its retries are step 10).
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/integration/test_pipeline.py -q` → 18 passed and one new gateway test; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **1561 passed** (1542 plus 18 and 1). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repo: 20 mutants (the basis check and gate, words, candidate pages, the basis passed on, the statistics, the differs flag, the document tag, the logged counts, the failure log, the stage label, the cache directory, the ceiling, the call counter); all caught after five tests were added (an invalid basis refused before the file is read, a non-candidate page, the token ceiling, the logged ignored and unplaced counts, and the stage of a basis failure). Two were equivalent or left to 9b: the call counter's position relative to a raising provider, and the basis passed to `build_field_set` (a document with both bases is a 9b scenario).
- [ ] Phase 2 step 9b: end-to-end scenarios (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; synthetic PDFs and a scripted fake provider only, `private/` untouched, no live call, no network**): `tests/integration/test_pipeline_scenarios.py` (30 tests) and a larger `tests/integration/pipeline_fixture.py` (a two-basis PDF with a consolidated page 1 and a standalone page 2, a narrative page, and reply builders). Scenarios: a clean reply; the model's mistakes (Example 1: the comparative figure and the note reference for the current period; Example 2: a bracketed figure without its brackets, and with them as a valid negative; Example 3: an undeclared scale; a label of another field; a quote not on the page; a missing currency attribute blocking every field; an empty reply, where every field is `not_found` and the run does not fail, FR-011.5); invalid output with the one corrective retry, a reply naming the derived field, two invalid replies failing with `E-LLM-INVALID-OUTPUT` and the raw reply attached, an invalid first reply not being cached while the corrected one is, and the token ceiling stopping the retry; a page range on the statement page, on a page with no title (`E-EXTRACT-BASIS-UNAVAILABLE`, no call) and outside the document (`E-INPUT-PAGE-RANGE`); a document with both bases run once with each configured basis (B.6 row 10: the other basis's fields are set aside, the bases present are recorded, the model's basis attribute is flagged as differing, and the two are never reconciled into a conflict), a standalone-only document, and custom locator settings reaching every stage; the cache key (a changed prompt version, model, output limit, candidate pages or document is a miss; the cache directory given is the one written); and reproducibility (two fresh caches and a cache hit give identical serialised results, and a fresh process gives `tests/fixtures/pipeline_field_set_golden.json`, generated by the test's own child script and reviewed). Covers the Phase 2 requirement that the pipeline runs end to end offline with the fake provider (FR-003.6) and B.6 rows 9, 10 and 14.
  - Notes: (1) the serialised field set is a test-side stand-in for `results.json` (Phase 5); (2) the golden records the synthetic PDF's SHA-256 in `source_document`, and reportlab output is byte-stable only for the pinned version in `uv.lock`, so a reportlab upgrade needs the golden regenerated in a reviewed change (the floats in the validation details are from this machine's pdfplumber, as in B-120 and the word golden); (3) the run's elapsed time is logged but never stored in the result, so it cannot make two runs differ; (4) the token ceiling test relies on the prompt's size, so a long prompt edit may need its number adjusted.
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/integration/test_pipeline_scenarios.py -q` → 30 passed; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **1591 passed** (1561 plus 30). Mutation sweep across the whole pipeline on scratch copies outside the repo, run against the pipeline test files: 18 mutants (the basis passed to the gate, to extraction and to the field set, the page range, the candidate pages, the cache directory, the prompt argument, the ceiling, the attributes and fields verified, the locator settings to verification and to the attributes, the source document, the retry's cache key, the user text); all caught after four tests were added (a standalone-only document, custom locator settings through every stage, and the cache directory used).
- [ ] Phase 2 step 10 plan (2026-10-19; saved at the owner's request; **a plan, not an approval of later sub-steps or of any gated action**): live enablement, split into offline work and gated actions. **Part 1, offline, built unstaged and committed on the owner's instruction:** **10a** the OpenAI provider behind `LLMProvider`, tested against a fake client with no network (request with temperature 0, the output limit and the JSON-schema structured-output mode; reply text and reported usage; transient failures (timeout, rate limit, 5xx, 408, 409) raised as a retryable error; an authentication or permission failure as `E-LLM-AUTH`, never retried; other client errors as an internal error; the key never in a log line, exception, cache entry or `repr`; needs `uv add openai`); **10b** retry with exponential backoff (FR-011.3: 3 attempts, then `E-LLM-UNAVAILABLE`; injectable sleep; the two new error codes); **10c** safety and settings (`.env.example` with placeholder names only; a provider factory reading `OPENAI_API_KEY` from the process environment and failing with `E-LLM-AUTH` if absent; a pure cost-estimate function with price settings unset until pricing is verified; opt-in live tests, skipped by default, and a test that the default suite makes no network access). **Part 2, gated actions in order, each needing the owner's explicit approval:** **G1** D-02 verification (model availability, structured-output support and current pricing from OpenAI's public documentation, with sources and dates recorded; nothing is recorded that was not read); **G2** SEC-10 (the owner reviews OpenAI's API data-usage and retention terms and the result is recorded here); **G3** the owner sets `OPENAI_API_KEY` in their own environment (never in Git, never pasted into the session), chooses the token ceiling and confirms the model; **G4** the first live call, one small call on a synthetic PDF with a small ceiling and the maximum spend shown first; **G5** the tuning-set run, counts only, which reads the tuning PDFs in `private/` and sends their candidate pages to OpenAI (public annual reports, allowed by SEC-10) and needs separate explicit authorisation for the read and the send. The held-out selection (OQ-3) is still needed for the Phase 2 exit and must be made before any held-out document is inspected. Defaults taken (owner: "yes, save the plan and start 10a", after the recommendation to take all six): (1) `uv add openai` with its licence and new dependencies recorded; (2) the provider converts the Pydantic JSON schema to OpenAI's strict form (every property required, optional ones nullable, no extra properties, unsupported validation keywords removed since Pydantic still validates every reply), and the converter's version is part of the cache key's structured-output mode (`json_schema.v1`); (3) 3 attempts with waits of 1 s then 2 s, no random jitter, a failed attempt that returns no usage adds zero tokens and the ceiling check runs before every attempt; (4) no `.env` loader, the key comes from the process environment only; (5) price settings have no defaults and are filled only after G1, and the estimated cost goes into the manifest in Phase 5; (6) live tests are opt-in by an environment variable and a pytest marker.
- [ ] Phase 2 step 10a: the OpenAI provider (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; no network, no key, no live call: the SDK client is replaced by a fake object in every test**): `uv add openai` (openai 3.35.1, Apache-2.0; new dependencies httpx2 2.14.0 and httpcore2 2.14.0 BSD-3-Clause, h11 0.16.0 MIT, anyio 4.15.1 MIT, idna 3.20 BSD-3-Clause, sniffio 1.3.1 MIT OR Apache-2.0, jiter 0.17.0 MIT, truststore 0.10.5 MIT; licences read from the installed package metadata, not legal review; `httpx2` also declares `httpx2-jsfetch`, which was not installed on this platform). The SDK is a newer major version than I knew, so every call was written against the installed package, not from memory. `llm/openai_provider.py`: `OpenAIProvider(api_key, *, timeout_s=120, client=None)`, one chat-completions call per request with `temperature=0`, `max_completion_tokens`, the strict JSON-schema `response_format` and `store=False` (the completion is not retained, SEC-10); the SDK's own retries are off (`max_retries=0`) because retry and backoff are 10b's. Failures: a timeout or connection failure, 408, 409, 429 and any 5xx raise `LLMTransientError`; an authentication or permission failure raises `IngestionError` `E-LLM-AUTH` (new code, never retried) without its cause, because the provider's message can quote part of the key; anything else the provider refuses raises `LLMRequestError` (internal, not catalogued) with only the HTTP status. The key goes to the SDK client and nowhere else (not an attribute, not the `repr`, a message, a log line or the cache). A refusal or missing content is empty text (it then fails validation and gets the corrective retry); a missing usage is a conservative estimate. `llm/strict_schema.py`: the pure converter from the Pydantic JSON schema to the provider's strict form (every property required, optional ones nullable, objects closed, unsupported validation keywords removed since Pydantic still validates every reply), with `ADAPTER_VERSION = "1"`; `STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_MODE` is now `json_schema.v1`, so a change to the converter is a cache miss. `llm/errors.py` holds the two exception types. Rows B-224 and B-225.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) `store=False` on every request, so the provider does not retain completions (SEC-10 asks only that data be public or synthetic; this is the more conservative reading of its retention concern); (2) `insufficient_quota` arrives as a 429 and is treated as transient, so it is retried and then reported as unavailable, which is wasteful but harmless; (3) a 400, 404 or 422 is an internal error rather than `E-LLM-UNAVAILABLE`, because the catalogue lists only timeout, rate limit and server errors under it; (4) the request timeout is a constructor argument (120 s), not a setting, as the PRD gives none; (5) the strict-schema rules follow the provider's documented strict mode as I know it and are **unconfirmed against its current documentation until G1 and the first live call (G4)**; (6) the system prompt embeds the full Pydantic schema as well as the provider receiving the strict one, which duplicates tokens but keeps the prompt provider-independent.
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_strict_schema.py tests/unit/test_openai_provider.py tests/integration/test_openai_provider_pipeline.py -q` → 13, 33 and 4 passed; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **1641 passed** (1591 plus 50). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repo: 21 mutants (temperature, `store`, the output limit, the SDK's retries, the permission mapping, the auth cause, the transient status set and 409, the request error's cause, the refusal rule, the usage fallback, the choices check, the mode check, the key check, the `repr`, and six in the converter: closing objects, requiring all properties, nullable optionals, dropped keywords, name maps, double null); all caught after three tests were added (the request error must not keep the provider's text, a refusal wins over content, nested objects without the flag are closed).
- [ ] Phase 2 step 10b: retry with exponential backoff (2026-10-19; **implemented, uncommitted at the time of writing; no network, no key, no live call, no real waiting: every test injects the sleep**): `llm/retry.py`. `RetryingProvider(provider, *, attempts=3, base_delay_s=1.0, factor=2.0, sleep=None)` wraps any provider (FR-011.3). A transient failure (`LLMTransientError`) is tried again up to `attempts` attempts in all, with waits of 1 s then 2 s (`base * factor ** (n - 1)`, no random jitter, no wait after the last attempt); after the last it raises `IngestionError` `E-LLM-UNAVAILABLE` (new code; safe message; the last reason, an HTTP status or a word, as its detail; the transient error as its cause). Nothing else is retried: `E-LLM-AUTH`, a refused request and every other error pass straight through on the first attempt. The wrapper sits inside the gateway, so the token-ceiling check runs once for the logical call and only the reply that arrives is recorded and cached; a failed attempt returned no usage and adds no tokens. `RetryingProvider.retries` counts retried attempts, and `RunStats.llm_retries` carries it into the run statistics (FR-012.1). Each retry is a WARNING line on `credit_intel.llm` with the attempt number, the reason and the wait, and a final line on giving up; never the request, the reply or the key. Covers FR-011.3 and the `E-LLM-UNAVAILABLE` row of the catalogue. Rows B-226.
  - Choices made in this step that the PRD leaves open (owner to confirm or change): (1) the ceiling is checked once per logical call and only the successful reply's usage is counted, so a billed-but-failed attempt, if the provider ever billed one, is not counted (the API reports no usage for a failed request); (2) there is no cap on a single wait (with the defaults the longest is 2 s); (3) a rate-limit reply's `Retry-After` is not used, because FR-011.3 specifies plain exponential backoff; (4) the retry wrapper is composed by the caller (step 10c's factory will do it), not hard-wired into the OpenAI provider; (5) the wrapper never logs at INFO, only WARNING, so the INFO stage log of FR-012.3 is unchanged.
  - Verified 2026-10-19 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/unit/test_retry.py -q` → 29 passed, plus 3 new pipeline scenario tests and one changed in the extraction flow; `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **1673 passed** (1641 plus 32). Mutation checks on scratch copies outside the repo: 17 mutants (the default attempts, first wait and factor, the exponent, the wait after the last attempt, the counter, catching everything, the error's cause and detail, both argument checks, sleeping, the default sleep, both log lines, the statistics, the error code); all caught. An earlier sweep failed to collect because a text replacement also changed an import line inside the child-process script of the scenario test; it was found, restored and the sweep rerun, so none of the numbers above come from the broken run.

The worst case at any ceiling is bounded by `ceiling x $0.60 / 1,000,000` (all tokens priced as output), so a ceiling of 200,000 tokens cannot cost more than **$0.12**, and one of 400,000 cannot cost more than $0.24. SC-11 stays provisional until a real run is measured (G4, G5).
  - **Structured Outputs (D-02 "structured-output support").** `response_format` with `json_schema` "is only supported with the gpt-4o-mini, gpt-4o-mini-2024-07-18, and gpt-4o-2024-08-06 model snapshots and later". **Confirmed against this project's converter (`llm/strict_schema.py`):** "all fields or function parameters must be specified as `required`"; "we require developers to set `additionalProperties: false`"; "it is possible to emulate an optional parameter by using a union type with `null`" (the page's examples use `["string", "null"]`); "the root level object of a schema must be an object, and not use `anyOf`" (ours is an object); definitions and `$ref` to `#/$defs/...` are supported; supported types are string, number, boolean, integer, object, array, enum and `anyOf`; limits are up to 5000 object properties in total, up to 10 levels of nesting, 120,000 characters in total for property, definition and enum names, and 1000 enum values (ours is about 30 properties, 3 levels, a few dozen enum values). Not supported: `allOf`, `not`, `dependentRequired`, `dependentSchemas`, `if`, `then`, `else` (the converter emits none). **A refusal "does not necessarily follow the schema"** and arrives in a `refusal` field, which the provider already treats as empty text. **Keyword findings that differ from my earlier assumption:** the guide lists `pattern`, `format`, `minimum`, `maximum`, `exclusiveMinimum`, `exclusiveMaximum`, `multipleOf`, `minItems` and `maxItems` as supported properties, and lists `minLength`, `maxLength`, `pattern`, `format`, the numeric bounds and `minItems`/`maxItems` as unsupported "for fine-tuned models only". The converter removes all of these, which is safe in either case (a looser schema; Python still validates every reply), so **no code change is needed**; the guide does not mention `default`, `title` or `description` in its keyword lists, and the converter removes those too. The guide also notes that "the first request you make with any schema will have additional latency". **Not found on the pages read:** what `finish_reason: "length"` looks like on Chat Completions (the guide speaks only of an incomplete response and a max-tokens limit), so the truncation handling (an invalid reply, then the corrective retry) rests on the project's own design. **The strict-schema rules are therefore confirmed against the documentation but still untested against the live API until G4.**
  - **SEC-10 inputs for the owner's review (G2; the review itself and its record are the owner's).** The your-data guide states that as of March 1, 2023 "data sent to the OpenAI API is not used to train or improve OpenAI models" unless the customer explicitly opts in. Abuse-monitoring logs are kept "up to 30 days" for all API feature usage, including `/v1/chat/completions` (longer where the law requires it or it is "reasonably necessary to protect our services or any third party from harm"). Zero Data Retention is available to approved customers, per organisation or project, for `/v1/chat/completions` ("Yes, see below for limitations"); with it enabled, "the `store` parameter will always be treated as `false`". The provider already sends `store=False`. Exceptions the page lists: prompt-cache key/value tensors may be kept in GPU-local storage for up to 24 hours; image and file inputs are scanned and flagged content is retained for manual review; data residency limits apply outside the US. **Consequence for SEC-10:** even with `store=False`, request content is held by OpenAI for up to 30 days for abuse monitoring unless ZDR is enabled, so the rule that only public or synthetic documents are sent is not optional, and the tuning-set run (G5) may send only the public annual reports.

This is a finding about the **prompt**, not the verifier: FR-005.1 allows no fuzzy matching, so the remedy is in the wording (for example, requiring the whole row from its label to its last figure), and a change needs a new prompt version (`extraction-v2`, a cache miss by design). It is not applied; the prompt wording is still awaiting the owner's review, and a change is best tried on synthetic and tuning documents only (O.2). (3) **A bug of mine, found and fixed:** the model returned `entity_name` with `period_role: "current"` (strict mode makes the role nullable for every attribute), and `verify_attributes` silently dropped it, so `entity_name` came out `not_found`. Had the same happened to the basis, currency or scale attribute, every field would have been blocked. Only a period-end has a role now; any role on another attribute is ignored and not stored. 4 new test functions (11 cases) in `tests/unit/test_document_attributes.py`; the recorded reply's attribute test failed before the fix and passes after it. A period-end with a null role is still not used (which period it is cannot be guessed), which is a known limit and is tested.

The recorded v1 reply and its replay tests are unchanged (a recording is a fact about what v1 produced). **Size effect, by the same arithmetic as the G3 preflight:** the system prompt grows from 6,209 to 6,660 characters; the project's pessimistic input estimate for the synthetic page rises from 3,262 to 3,487 tokens, one call reserves 7,583 tokens (7,358 before), and the worst case at the G1 prices is $0.00298 for one call and $0.00596 for two (about $0.0029 and $0.0059 before), so the earlier conclusions stand; the measured live input was 2,060 tokens with v2 against 1,937 with v1 (+123, +6.3%). No approval of any step is recorded here, `private/` has not been read for it, and nothing has been sent to OpenAI).** G5 runs the whole pipeline on the two public tuning reports and records **counts only, no accuracy claims** (the Phase 2 exit wording). **Facts used, all already recorded above (B-133 evidence, which predates the Stage A locator changes and may no longer hold):** R1 Infosys Integrated Annual Report 2024-25, 369 pages, Ind AS, ₹ crore, standalone statements on physical pages 194-197 and consolidated on 274-277, the standalone titles carrying no basis word; R2 Microsoft FY2025 financial statements, 80 pages, US GAAP, USD millions, income statements p.36 and balance sheets p.38, titles naming no basis.
  - **Two findings that shape the plan (reasoned from the recorded facts and the code; neither is measured yet).** (1) **R2 will most likely stop at the basis gate with `E-EXTRACT-BASIS-UNAVAILABLE`, before any LLM call**: the configured basis is consolidated, R2's titles name no basis, so no candidate page names the configured basis. That is open decision **OD-10** (how a statement whose title names no basis is treated); it costs nothing and is itself a recordable finding, but extraction on R2 cannot be tested unless OD-10 is decided, and the assistant will not decide it. R1's standalone pages will give `basis_unknown` fields, as designed. (2) **R1 risks truncated output**: with both standalone and consolidated statements among the candidates the model may return fields for both bases, perhaps 4,000 to 5,000 tokens of JSON against the current `max_output_tokens` of 4,096; a truncated reply is invalid JSON, fails validation twice and ends in `E-LLM-INVALID-OUTPUT`. `gpt-4o-mini` allows 16,384 output tokens (G1), so the plan proposes 8,192 for G5, supplied as `CREDIT_INTEL_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (the shipped file unchanged), which raises each call's reservation by 4,096 tokens.
  - **Part A, offline preparation, no approvals needed.** A1: an opt-in live runner, `tests/live/test_live_tuning.py`, taking the document path from an environment variable, running the full pipeline and printing **counts only** (candidate page numbers, statuses by reason, attribute statuses, tokens, cost), never document text, quotes or values; tested offline on a synthetic PDF. A2: settle the `max_output_tokens` value.
  - **Part B, steps that each need the owner's explicit approval, in order.** **B1, offline dry run** (no network, no key): ingest and locate on R1 and R2 only and report page counts, the candidate pages, the character count of those pages and whether the basis gate passes, so the ceiling and the maximum spend are computed from real sizes; it needs the owner's **authorisation to read the two PDFs in `private/`** (nothing else there: no logs, no labels) and their exact paths. **B2, three separate owner approvals:** the maximum spend (computed from B1); sending the candidate pages of R1, and of R2 if it passes the gate, to OpenAI under the G2 rule; the ceiling and output limit. **B3**, the owner runs each document in the owner's own terminal, one at a time, with the key loaded from the owner's `.env` into that terminal only; the assistant never sees the key. **B4**, the assistant records the counts here (with the cost and any failure code) and claims no accuracy.
  - **Part C, after G5.** Any change to the prompt, the locator or verification phrase lists, or a setting is made **only from these tuning documents** (PRD O.2) and on the owner's word. The owner may spot-check a few extracted values against the rendered pages as for B-133; comparing with the owner's label files would need its own authorisation to read `private/`.
  - **Provisional spend, assumptions until B1:** up to 15 candidate pages (the locator's cap) at up to 2,500 tokens each by the project's pessimistic estimate plus the 3,500-token prompt is about 41,000 input tokens; with 8,192 reserved output a call reserves about 49,000 and two calls (the one corrective retry) about 98,000; **proposed ceiling 120,000 tokens**, whose worst-case bound (everything priced as output at $0.60 per million) is **$0.072**; a realistic call (about 20,000 real input and 4,000 output tokens) costs about **$0.005**; a maximum-spend approval of **$0.10** would cover G5 with room. The G1 prices apply. Real figures come from B1.
  - **Not done, and not to be done without the owner:** no held-out document is touched; nothing in `private/` is read beyond the two tuning PDFs, and nothing without authorisation; the key is never seen by the assistant; document text, quotes and values are not printed, logged or pasted; the response cache `.cache/llm/` would hold the page text of the run (the accepted G2 risk) and is cleared by deleting the directory.
  - **Open questions put to the owner (unanswered at the time of writing):** (1) approve the plan as written, or what to change; (2) OD-10: leave open (R2 stops at the gate and that is the recorded finding) or decide it; (3) agree to `max_output_tokens` 8,192 for G5; (4) authorise the B1 read of R1 and R2 in `private/` and give the exact file paths. **Until these are answered, none of B1 to B4 may start.**

New package `src/credit_intel/evaluation/`: **`guard.py`** enforces the owner's G2 rule in code: `check_may_send(sha256)` returns the neutral document ID only if the document's last record in `tests/eval/assignments.jsonl` is `tuning`; a held-out document, an unrecorded document, and a document moved from tuning to held out are refused before any request; the guard fails closed (a missing, unreadable, non-UTF-8 or malformed record, or a line with members out of order or an unknown set, refuses every document, not just the bad line's), a refusal shows at most a 12-character hash and no path, and a `synthetic=True` exemption exists for tests that build their own documents (a test checks the live runners never pass it). **`summary.py`**: counts-only summaries (candidate page numbers, fields by status, reason and basis, one `field/period/basis: status (reason)` line per present record, attribute statuses, attempts, calls, retries, tokens, cost) with no text, quote, label, value, file name or path; a run that stops on a catalogued error is a result with its code and detail; `to_line` gives one ASCII line with sorted keys. **`runner.py`**: `run_tuning_document` checks the guard first, then runs the pipeline and returns the summary (a catalogued stop such as `E-EXTRACT-BASIS-UNAVAILABLE` is returned, not raised; an unreadable file is `E-INPUT-NOT-FOUND`). **`dry_run.py`** (`uv run --locked python -m credit_intel.evaluation.dry_run <pdf> [--basis] [--pages]`): the B1 dry run, offline with no key and no model call; it reports page counts, candidate pages and their sizes, the basis gate result, the project's conservative input estimate for the real candidate pages, the reservation per call and for two calls, a suggested ceiling (twice the reservation, rounded up to whole ten thousands), and the worst-case spend at the configured prices (`CREDIT_INTEL_PRICE_*`), and it holds only numbers, never text. **`tests/live/test_live_tuning.py`**: the opt-in live runner for one tuning document (`CREDIT_INTEL_DOCUMENT`, optional `CREDIT_INTEL_BASIS` and `CREDIT_INTEL_PAGES`), skipped by default like the smoke test, printing one `tuning run:` line. The suite now has two live tests, both skipped by default.
  - **Found while testing, fixed:** the dry run first raised a raw `FileNotFoundError` for a missing file; it now gives the catalogued `E-INPUT-NOT-FOUND` (the dry run hashes the file through ingestion, the runner converts the error, and the message names no path). **Choices:** (1) the guard enforces the G2 rule only where this project's live runners call it; the application itself does not refuse other documents, because Release 1 analysts bring their own documents and SEC-10 is an operating rule; (2) the live tuning runner uses the default response cache `.cache/llm` so a repeat costs nothing, which keeps the real page text on disk (the accepted G2 risk; delete the directory to clear it); (3) the summary names fields and statuses but not values, so it can be pasted; comparing values with the owner's labels is not done here.
  - Verified 2026-10-20 by running: `uv run --locked pytest tests/ -q` → **1805 passed, 2 skipped** (1759 plus 46: 22 guard tests and 24 runner, summary and dry-run tests; the two skips are the live tests). Mutation checks on a scratch copy outside the repo: 18 mutants (held-out, unknown and synthetic permitted; the first record winning; member order, unknown sets and bad JSON not refusing; an unreadable record read as empty; a full hash in the message; only the first chunk hashed; the guard skipped; a stop raised; a raw OS error; a cost shown without prices; a value leaked into the outcomes; the error text instead of its detail; a ceiling covering one call; the reservation without the output; the wrong price for the bound); all caught after two tests were added (an unknown set on one line must refuse the documents on good lines, and an unreadable record must raise).

In this run it failed safe (the claimed period end did not match the columns). - [x] **Phase 3 step 3a: the derivation core and EBIT (2026-10-24; unstaged; offline).** New package `src/credit_intel/calculations/` with `derived.py`: `derive_ebit(operating_profit, profit_before_tax, interest_expense)` and `derive_ebit_for(field_set, role)`. A derived record has `origin = derived`, a derivation `{method_id, formula, input_field_ids}`, and no evidence of its own; it takes the inputs' basis, role and (when they agree) period end. **Method (H.5 rule 3, fixed):** `EBIT-OP` whenever operating profit has a record that is not `not_found`, whatever its status; `EBIT-PBT` otherwise. **Status (rule 2):** `extracted` only if every input is; else `not_found` (reason `missing_input`), then `conflicting`, then `requires_review` (the input's reason). A missing input is never zero. **Rule 1:** different currencies give `requires_review` with `currency_mismatch`; different period ends give `requires_review` with `period_mismatch` (my reading: the PRD names no reason for it); inputs of different bases or roles raise `ValueError` (a caller error). Values are exact `Decimal`. **Two interpretations for the owner to confirm:** (1) the field model requires at least two candidates for a `conflicting` record and a derived value has no evidence of its own, so a conflicting derived EBIT lists the **combined candidate results**, each carrying its inputs' evidence side by side (quotes joined with " | ", labels with " + ", raw values with " + "); (2) where an input is `conflicting` but another has no value (`requires_review`), the combined candidates cannot be listed, so the result is `requires_review` with that other input's reason. Tests B-239 (23 in `tests/unit/test_derived_ebit.py`: both methods, every non-extracted status, precedence, currency and period, the comparative period, a field set, a 300-case seeded property test); seven mutants caught (fallback by quality, minus for plus, conflicting before not_found, no currency check, no period check, reason lost, context ignored). Suite 1925 passed, 2 skipped. **Not yet:** total borrowings (3b, OD-9) and the metrics (3c to 3e).

Arithmetic uses one fixed `Decimal` context (28 digits, ties to even) so a result does not depend on the caller's global context; rounding for display is left to the memo (Phase 5). **My readings of the PRD, for the owner:** the order of the checks (status, currency, period, then edge cases) and the precedence among unusable inputs are not stated in Section I and follow the H.5 order; a current revenue below zero in MET-01 gives `input_requires_review` (the PRD says "through FR-006.5"); inputs of different bases, and arguments in the wrong period roles, raise `ValueError` as caller errors. Tests B-240 (32 in `tests/unit/test_metrics_growth_margin.py`, with a shared helper `tests/unit/calc_records.py`; `test_derived_ebit.py` now uses the same helper); all PRD acceptance tests for MET-01 and MET-02 are in; twelve mutants caught. The final flush of the parent directory is best effort, because by then the run is complete and visible. `export_run` enters `memo_generated`, then `exported` **before** building the manifest, so the manifest can record `exported` as its final state; if the write then fails, the recorder shows `failed` in `exported` with `E-EXPORT-FAILED`. **Fault injection:** every operating-system step of a write is failed in turn, and a child process is killed at every step; no run folder is ever left incomplete. - [x] **Phase 5 Part 2, gate round: preparation (2026-10-25; unstaged; offline; no real document opened).** The owner asked to prepare the gate round. **Built:** `src/credit_intel/evaluation/gate.py` (`load_reference`, `compare`, `replay`, `evaluate_gate`, and a command, `python -m credit_intel.evaluation.gate`) and `docs/gate_round_guide.md`. The reference file is the one the owner's condition of 2026-10-24 describes: six hand-checked values per tuning report (profit before tax, interest expense, total borrowings; current and comparative), written from the rendered pages and kept in `private/eval/`. The tool replays the model's reply **from the response cache** (the provider refuses to be called, so nothing is sent and nothing costs), verifies it as a normal run would, and prints **counts only**: `match`, `mismatch` or `missing` per value, with a reason code. **An extracted value that differs from its reference fails the gate**, as does a value that is not extracted. The gate passes only if every tuning document on the assignment record was replayed and matched on all six values; a held-out document is refused. Tests B-274 (55), B-275 (15), B-276 (5); sixteen mutants of `gate.py` caught (one survivor drove one added test). Suite 2985 passed, 2 skipped. **Readings for the owner:** (1) "borrowings" is the **total the metrics use**: a reported total, or the sum of non-current and current borrowings (plus a separate current-maturities row where H.5 adds it); a total the report does not print is given as `parts` and the expected value is their sum (your decision (b)); (2) the page is informational: a right value from another page is a match with a flag; (3) a record of the other basis is never used, so a value read from the wrong statement shows as `missing`; (4) a cache miss is a failure to replay, never a pass; (5) the reference has no free-text field, so it cannot carry a quote or a label; (6) the gate command uses its own refusal rule (tuning documents only, as for a send), not the product command line's `--confirm-public-document`.

**The Part 2 gate path, proposed (a plan, not an approval of any step; every live step needs the owner's separate approvals of spend, pages and limits, and is run in the owner's own terminal):**
- **G1 (yours):** write `private/eval/R1_reference.json` and `private/eval/R2_reference.json` from the rendered pages, and check each with `--check-reference`. I do not read them.

The `gfm-like` preset was tried and not used: it turns on link detection, which needs a further package, and auto-detected links would also hide a link in the memo. **Built:** `tests/unit/test_memo_markdown.py` (80 tests) and `tests/integration/test_memo_markdown_run.py` (3). Every memo the suite generates (the golden run, four scenarios, an asserted-basis run, and the memo the command writes to disk) is parsed and checked: the title and nine section headings in order, rectangular tables, no raw HTML, link, image or strikethrough, hard breaks inside indicator blocks, identifiers as code spans; and 19 hostile strings (headings, table cells, HTML, links, images, emphasis, list and quote markers, rules, entities, backslashes, control characters) are put into the profile, the entity name, a rule name and an evidence quote, and must come out as the text they were, in every place, with no change to the headings, tables or lists. **A real defect found and fixed:** text that begins a quote line, such as an evidence quote starting `1. `, `- ` or `---`, became a list item or a rule inside the memo, because the escape covered `#`, `>` and `*` but not those markers. New `esc_start` (in `memo/text.py`) escapes a leading `-`, `=` or list number and is used for the three evidence-quote lines only, where document text starts a line. **Reading for the owner:** a parser cannot tell a forged origin label from a real one once rendered, because the visible text is the same (`\[SOURCE: p.9\]` shows as `[SOURCE: p.9]`); the safeguard is the raw-text check, which counts unescaped brackets, and the parser test shows that no extra label-looking text appears beyond the copies of the hostile text itself. FR-010.2's \"cannot become an origin label\" is therefore proved by both checks together, not by the parser alone. **Mutants:** the escape set was weakened one character at a time against the wider memo tests; `<`, `>`, `#`, `|`, `\\`, `[` `]`, `+`, `&`, `_`, `*` and the backtick are all caught; `~` is caught once the strikethrough rule is on; `!` is equivalent (it only matters before `[`, which is escaped). Tests B-285 and B-286. Suite **3206 passed, 2 skipped**. **Next:** the attribute IDs and the new catalogue code (then S3, the check before export). No real document was read and nothing live was run.

A refusal at the door would need a skew measure, a new error code and a PRD change, and was not built; the owner can ask for it. Documented in the README, in the memo's section 9 for runs that used OCR (`OCR_SCOPE`: skewed or rotated pages are not corrected; OCR has not been tuned or measured on real scanned reports) and in `docs/d15_tesseract_findings.md` section 7. (2) **licence:** the findings say the installed files do not state the English data's licence, and record, as external and unverified, that Tesseract and its English data are typically Apache 2.0 upstream, **flagged for the owner's confirmation** (not settled). (3) **README "There is no OCR":** the bullet now keeps the sentence the memo's fixed text must contain and adds that it is the default and the whole of Release 1, and that OCR is in progress, off unless `--ocr` with `--pages`, English only, never accepted as extracted, not correcting skew or rotation, tried on synthetic scans only. **Not changed, and why:** the memo's fixed text for runs without OCR still says "Only text-based PDF files are read. (4) **Release 1 item 9 gap closed:** a new bullet in the README and memo section 9 (word for word, as the README test requires) names that non-current borrowings, current borrowings, current maturities and operating profit have often not been found on real annual reports, so debt-to-equity is not defined without a total or its components, and EBIT then comes from profit before tax plus interest expense; `tests/fixtures/memo_golden.md` gained that one line. New `ingestion/tesseract_ocr.py`, the only module that runs Tesseract: `TesseractExtractor` (name `tesseract`; version read once from `tesseract --version`; the English data checked with `--list-langs`; either failing, or a command that is missing or cannot be run, is `E-INPUT-OCR-UNAVAILABLE`). Each requested page is rendered in memory at `ocr.dpi` with pypdfium2 and passed as PNG on standard input to `tesseract stdin stdout -l eng --dpi N tsv` with `OMP_THREAD_LIMIT=1`; no file is written (FR-001A.6). The TSV is read into word boxes (pixels converted to points), keeping word rows (level 5) with text and a confidence of 0 or more; lines are ordered by their numeric block, paragraph and line numbers, words by position. The time limit covers the whole call (a deadline), not each page. A non-zero exit, unreadable output, an unreadable PDF or a time-out is `E-INPUT-OCR-FAILED`; no engine output is carried into an error. `ingestion/ocr_engine.py`: the default engine is now Tesseract (the placeholder `UnavailableOcrExtractor` is gone); two tests that relied on 'no engine' now name an absent command explicitly, so they pass whatever is installed. `pyproject.toml`: the `real_ocr` marker, for the one opt-in test of the real engine (skipped unless Tesseract is installed and `CREDIT_INTEL_REAL_OCR=1`). **Decisions for the owner:** (1) no new package was added: Pillow and pypdfium2 are already locked as pdfplumber's dependencies, but the adapter now imports them directly, so I propose declaring them as direct dependencies (`uv add pypdfium2 pillow`; the lock would gain no new package) and have NOT done it; (2) PDF pages that are rotated are rendered upright by the PDF reader and the boxes are in the rendered page's frame (not tested with a rotated scan). **What this does not establish:** how well Tesseract reads anything, its error rate and time per page on synthetic scans, and the licence check of Tesseract and its English data (to be read from the installed files); D-15's 'verification required' is therefore OPEN. 37 tests run, 1 opt-in skipped. - [x] **Phase 7 plan written (2026-10-30; owner: "Start Phase 7 ... a plan first, not code"; unstaged; plan only, nothing built, `docs/PRD.md` unchanged at 0.4.61).** `docs/PHASE7_plan.md`: scope (Docker, deployment, persistence, authentication, TLS, the Section M review), what exists, a row-by-row review of SEC-01 to SEC-16 for a service reachable from outside, design by area, 13 open decisions OD-27 to OD-39 each with options, trade-offs and a proposal, an eight-step build order, risks. **Findings from reading the Phase 6 code (not yet fixed):** F-1 every child process that reads a PDF (parse worker, page render, report worker; Tesseract gets a copy of the whole environment) holds `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `CREDIT_INTEL_API_TOKEN`; F-2 a Docker build would copy `.env`, `private/`, `runs/` and the PDFs now in the repository root unless a `.dockerignore` stops it; F-3 prompts, risk rules, the send-rule record and the version lookup are found from the source-tree location, so a wheel-based image would fail every run; F-4 the resident-memory check calls `ps` and silently does nothing when `ps` is absent (a slim image), and a container memory limit below `parse_memory_mb` times the pool kills the container instead of ending one run; F-5 an upload is read to the end before the queue is asked for room (disk fill and held connections for an authenticated caller); also the token travels in clear over plain HTTP, one shared secret with no attribution, revocation or failed-attempt limit, any token holder reads every run, the origin check breaks behind a proxy that rewrites `Host`, no HSTS, runs lost at a restart, no retention, the git commit unknown in an image, results across platforms unshown. **Limits of this plan:** Docker is not installed on this machine, so the image cannot be built or run here; no provider was priced; "Expanded evaluation" in the PRD row is raised as OD-27 and not planned. - [x] **Phase 6 step 4: the web page (2026-10-30; owner: "commit it and step 4"; unstaged; offline; B-334).** Step 3 was committed first as `d411bb7`. New `api/pages.py` (server-rendered HTML, escaping through one helper, the memo through `memo_html`), `api/static/app.css` (both colour themes, phone width) and `api/static/app.js` (about 25 lines), and `app.py` now shares `accept`, `listing`, `run_status` and `read_memo` between the API and the pages. Pages: `GET /` (form and list), `POST /ui/submit`, `GET /ui/runs/{id}`, `GET /static/app.css`, `GET /static/app.js`; none is in the API document. **Decisions I took (flag if you disagree):** (1) *The token and the browser.* A browser cannot send a bearer header, so with a token set the pages answer `401` like every other endpoint (SEC-15 read literally); the page is for the loopback default, or behind a reverse proxy that adds the header. Options if you want it usable with a token: a login form that sets an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict cookie (a second way to authenticate, which SEC-15 does not have), or a token typed into the page and sent by the script (breaks the server-rendered memo). Not built. (2) The profile is a JSON textarea (the four allowed keys listed beside it) rather than four fields, so that the page posts exactly what `POST /runs` takes. (3) The memo is rendered with the report's parser plus `link`, `image`, `autolink` and the two raw-HTML rules off, on top of `html: False`; the memo check already forbids links and raw HTML, this is a second layer for a memo file that was edited on disk. (4) The confirmation checkbox is always shown with the notice, and is `required` in the form only when the configured provider is not the fake one; the server enforces the rule regardless. (5) The run view reloads itself when the run ends instead of patching the page, so the final page is the same server-rendered page a later visit sees. **Proof:** the earlier suite passes unchanged; 33 new tests in `test_api_pages.py`, 8 in `test_api_script.py` (the script run under Node against a stand-in page; skipped without Node). Mutation checks on `pages.py`, the script and the stylesheet: 40 mutants; all killed after adding tests for the survivors (a manifest whose text reaches the page unescaped; the script's timing, encoding, retry count and cache setting) except two equivalent ones (removing either raw-HTML rule alone, which `html: False` and the other rule cover). Suite: 4575 passed, 5 skipped. Not done: README, how to start it, the OpenAPI check and an end-to-end test of the whole path (step 5); PRD status cells.
- [x] **Phase 6 step 3: upload and exposure security (2026-10-30; owner: "commit it and step 3"; unstaged; offline; B-333).** Step 2 was committed first as `0ddc84d`. New `api/security.py` (a pure ASGI middleware, `is_loopback`, `check_exposure`), `api/__main__.py` (the launcher, also the `credit-intel-api` script), `config.load_api_token`. **Token (SEC-15):** from `CREDIT_INTEL_API_TOKEN` only (a `token` key in the settings file is an unknown key and is refused), at least 32 printable ASCII characters, else `E-CONFIG-INVALID` naming `api.token`; with a token, everything but `GET /health` needs `Authorization: Bearer ...` (scheme case-insensitive, comparison with `hmac.compare_digest`, fixed `401` body, `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`; not accepted in a URL, form field or cookie); the token never appears in a response or a log line (tested). **Start-up refusal:** `create_app` (and so the launcher) raises `E-CONFIG-INVALID` for a host that is not a loopback address with no token, for a bad token on any host, and when the system temporary directory is inside `api.output_dir` (uploads must stay outside the output folder, SEC-14); the launcher prints the catalogued message and exits 6 without listening. **Headers (SEC-16):** the four headers on every response, including `401`, `403`, `404`, `405`, `413`, `429`, `503` and the served files. **Logging (FR-016.12):** one INFO line per request (method, the service's own path shape or `-`, status); the server's own access log is switched off so the raw request line is not repeated. **Disconnect:** a client that goes away mid-upload leaves no file and no run (tested at the ASGI level). **Addition not in the PRD text, for the owner to keep or drop:** a `POST` whose `Origin` header names another host than the one the request was sent to is refused `403` (fixed body). Reason: without a token, a loopback service can be reached by a form on any website open in the user's browser (a multipart `POST` needs no preflight), which could start a paid run; the same-origin form of step 4 and any non-browser client (no `Origin` header) are unaffected. **Other small choices:** only `GET /health` is exempt from the token (not `HEAD`); `/health` shows counts only. **Open point for step 4:** a plain HTML form cannot send a bearer header, so when a token is set the web page needs a way to carry it (for example the script adds the header from a token typed into the page and kept only in memory); the PRD does not say, and I will propose it before building. **Proof:** the earlier suite passes unchanged; 59 new tests in `tests/integration/test_api_security.py`; mutation checks on the new code (26 mutants of the token, health exemption, origin, headers, loopback test, start-up refusal, token loader and launcher; all killed after adding tests for two survivors: a loose `/health` prefix test, and the server's access-log setting). Suite: 4534 passed, 5 skipped. Not done: the web page (step 4); README, a documented start and an end-to-end test (step 5); PRD status cells.
- [x] **Phase 6 step 2: the API core (2026-10-30; owner: "commit it, step 2"; unstaged; offline; B-332).** New `src/credit_intel/api/` (`app.py`, `jobs.py`, `uploads.py`, `errors.py`), `[api]` in `config.py` (`ApiSettings`, `load_api_settings`) and `config/settings.toml` (1 / 4 / 1,200 as approved; `output_dir` and `cache_dir` left unset, absolute paths required, so the service cannot start until the owner sets them or the `CREDIT_INTEL_API_*` variables), the two API-only catalogue codes `E-RUN-TIMEOUT` and `E-API-REQUEST` (`exit_code` is now `int | None`; the command line never raises them), `service.check_send_rule` (extracted from `execute_run`, same behaviour), `TesseractExtractor.check_available`. **Endpoints:** exactly `POST /runs`, `GET /runs`, `GET /runs/{id}`, `GET /runs/{id}/{memo,results,manifest,report}`, `GET /health`; docs pages switched off. **Decisions I took where the PRD leaves room (flag if you disagree):** (1) the client's file name is reduced to a safe base name (`[A-Za-z0-9._ -]`, no directory, no leading dot, 100 characters) and used as the name of the file *inside* the random private directory, so the manifest records it as the command line records a file name; this reads SEC-14's "never used as a path" as "never joined to a path the client controls"; the alternative is a name override through `run_pipeline` and the ingestion layer. (2) The run ID is fixed at upload completion by giving the run's recorder that time for its first event; queue wait is therefore inside the run's timestamps. (3) Answers about the transport (`404`, `405`, `429`, `503`) have the body `{"code": null, "message": fixed}` and are not catalogue codes. (4) A running run is never shown as `exported` until its files are written (the recorder enters `exported` first): the status reports `memo_generated` until then. (5) A second submission of the same document in the same microsecond is refused as `E-API-REQUEST`. **Not in this step (step 3):** the bearer token and the refusal to start on a non-loopback address, response headers (CSP etc.), disconnect-during-upload and startup tests beyond those here, a launcher (`uvicorn` entry point), and the web page (step 4). **Proof:** the earlier suite passes with three edits (the catalogue tests now know the two API-only codes; the README exit-code test ignores codes with no exit code). New: 64 tests in `tests/integration/test_api.py`, 49 in `tests/unit/test_api_settings.py`, 2 added to the catalogue test. Mutation checks: 32 mutants of the API guards and 6 of the settings loader, all killed after tests were added for the survivors (the first-bytes signature refusal, the safe name, the duplicate-file and field limits, the upload cleanup on a refused request, the pre-retry deadline check, and the shutdown cleanup); one mutant was equivalent (a redundant run-ID check) and the redundancy was removed. Suite: 4475 passed, 5 skipped. **Dependencies added (SEC-11; reason: FR-016; pinned in `uv.lock`):** fastapi 0.162.0 (MIT), uvicorn 0.54.0 (BSD-3-Clause), python-multipart 0.0.32 (Apache-2.0); dev: httpx 0.28.1 (BSD-3-Clause); transitive: starlette 1.10.0 (BSD-3-Clause), click 8.5.0 (BSD-3-Clause), annotated-doc 0.0.5 (MIT), opentelemetry-api 1.45.2 (Apache-2.0), httpcore 1.0.9 (BSD-3-Clause), certifi 2026.10.23 (MPL-2.0). Licences were read from installed package metadata, not legal review. Next: step 3, on the owner's word.

- [x] **FIXED 2026-10-30 (owner chose option B: "list only the inputs that actually exist"; see the end of this entry). Was: a derived value cites input IDs that have no record, so the memo check stops the run (found 2026-10-30 in an unlogged failure of 2026-10-26; owner: "get it recorded in PROGRESS.md either way"; NOT fixed; flagged to the owner before the Phase 6 PRD draft).** *What failed:* run `20261026T112836.718054Z-a9e20d3e` (the 6-page quarterly results document `Q1 FY 2026-27.pdf`, `--pages 2 --basis standalone --assert-basis standalone`, made by `1d311b0` with a dirty tree) stopped at `memo_generated` with "the memo failed its traceability check (1 problem)", exit 1, a manifest-only failure record, no memo. It had no PROGRESS entry. *Reproduced with today's code* (2026-10-30), offline, from the response cache (no key, 0 tokens, 1 cache hit): same counts (7 fields proposed, 4 attributes, 4 requires_review, 3 unplaced, 1 derived) and the same single problem, `unknown field ID interest_expense.<date>.standalone` (digits masked; counts only, no figures recorded). *Cause:* `missing_fields` adds `not_found` placeholders only for periods whose end date is **validated**; here the current period end was `requires_review` (`period_mismatch`) and the comparative `not_found`, so `results.json` held four field records and no placeholders. `derive_ebit` (`calculations/derived.py`, `derive`) still builds `input_field_ids` from the date of the profit-before-tax record it does have, naming `interest_expense.<that date>.standalone`, which has no record; the memo's derived-values line cites it and K.10 rejects it. A neighbouring inconsistency: the interest-coverage metric's `input_field_ids` lists `interest_expense.<date>` without the basis suffix (not caught by the check's pattern). *Scope:* real and recurring in kind, not a one-off: any run whose period ends are unverified and that has a derived value (EBIT from PBT; the borrowings sum) with some but not all inputs present. Quarterly documents are out of scope (H.1.1), but the same path exists for an annual report with unverified period ends; the Reliance and R1 real runs passed because their period ends validated. *Safe failure:* the check did its job; no wrong memo was written. The results are what is inconsistent. *Not caused by, and unrelated to, the report work* (the memo check runs before the report is built). *Options (none chosen):* (A) create placeholder records so every named ID exists; (B) list only inputs that exist in `input_field_ids`; (C) use the role form already used in the memo's "Fields:" line (`interest_expense.current`) for inputs with no record. *Test:* `tests/unit/test_derived_inputs_exist.py` states the contract as `xfail(strict=True)` (it must fail today and will fail the suite, as an unexpected pass, when the defect is fixed, so the marker must then be removed), plus one plain test pinning the facts. **Fix (2026-10-30, option B):** `_derive` (`calculations/derived.py`) now lists in `input_field_ids` only the inputs that have a record, each by its own record's ID (so an input with a different period end is named correctly too); with no input at all it names the record it was anchored on (the derivation needs at least one). The `record_inputs` path (unused by any caller) is unchanged. `tests/unit/test_derived_inputs_exist.py` is now six ordinary tests (the expected-failure marker is gone). Mutation checks: naming all wanted inputs again fails 4 of 6; one mutant (`present or anchors` to `anchors`) was equivalent and became the code. Replay of the Q1 run from the cache with the fixed code: `memo_checked` finds 0 problems, exit 0. Suite: 4350 passed, 5 skipped, no expected failures. The interest-coverage metric's input IDs without the basis suffix (noted above) were not changed: not part of this fix, and the check does not flag them.

- [x] **PRD v0.4.44: OCR of scanned pages specified, implemented after Release 1 (2026-10-26; owner decision; specification only; nothing built).** The owner asked for OCR so that scanned annual reports (no text layer, refused today with `E-INPUT-NO-TEXT-LAYER`) can be processed. A proposal (`docs/PRD_ocr_proposal_draft.md`, `ce2a759`) set out the trigger, the engine choice, the effect on the label, period-column and numeric checks, a review flag and open items OD-11 to OD-18. Decisions (2026-10-26): OD-12 option (a) and OD-14 option (a) by the owner's choice, the rest as recommended, then "approve v0.4.44, implement after Release 1, OD-18 option b". **Why OCR is not treated like a text layer:** the checks compare the model's quote with the page text, and with OCR that text is itself a machine reading, so a misread digit would pass every check. An OCR field is therefore never `extracted`: `requires_review`, reason `ocr_unconfirmed`, excluded from every metric, derived value and rule, and labelled `[OCR p.N, confidence …]` in the memo. Applied to `docs/PRD.md` (commit `66ea7db`): FR-001.6; new FR-001A (off by default, `--ocr` and `--pages` required, image-only pages only, OCR page cap and time limit as required settings with no default); FR-005 criterion 11 (exact matching, separate OCR column settings); error codes `E-INPUT-OCR-UNAVAILABLE` and `E-INPUT-OCR-FAILED` (exit 3 both, chosen when applying); D-15 (Tesseract behind a replaceable `OcrExtractor`); OD-11 to OD-18 decided; scope and limitation text; Appendix B.6 examples 145 to 152; record `docs/PRD_v0.4.44_draft.md`. **Not done:** no code, no settings, no engine installed, no scan assigned. - [x] **PRD v0.4.27, step S3: every memo is checked before export (2026-10-25; unstaged; offline).** The traceability checker moved from `tests/unit/memo_trace.py` into the package as `credit_intel.memo.trace` (`problems(memo, results)` unchanged; the old test path is a one-line re-export so the six importing test files keep working and the tests exercise the shipped checker). `export_run` calls it right after `render_memo` and before `recorder.enter(MEMO_GENERATED)`: it logs `stage=memo_checked run=… problems=N` for every run, and any problem raises `MemoCheckFailed` (carries the count, never text), so nothing is written. The command prints `Internal error. Run ID: …. The memo failed its traceability check (N problem[s]).` and exits 1; the failure record is the existing manifest-only one, `failed` in stage `memo_generated` with no code (the first state not yet entered). **Decision:** the check runs before `memo_generated` so the recorded failed stage is the one the memo was being made in, and the error stays uncatalogued (an internal defect, not a user-fixable condition), as v0.4.27 specifies. Tests: B-289 (10 new, including example 60 by injecting a faulty memo); six mutants caught, one survivor (`1 problems` matched by substring) fixed with an exact-message assertion. Suite **3253 passed, 2 skipped**. README states the check under outputs.

- G.1: no CLI exit codes exist yet (FR-014, Phase 5); the seven implemented input-error messages do not yet include the "what the user should do" text
- FR-001.6 page-range case and FR-013: implemented in commit 4 (see above); SC-12 recall not yet measured

Licence notes:
- No copyleft licence found among runtime dependencies.
- pypdfium2's bundled native libraries (abseil, AGG 2.3, fast_float, FreeType, ICU, Little-CMS, libjpeg-turbo, OpenJPEG, libpng, llvm-libc, PDFium, simdutf, zlib) all carry permissive licences. FreeType's FTL requires a credit in documentation if the software is redistributed.
- reportlab (dev only) ships a sample font (DarkGarden) under GPL alongside its BSD code; we do not use that font, and reportlab is never a runtime dependency.
- Licences were read from installed package metadata, not legal review.

## Decisions
- `uv init --bare` was used so no placeholder application code (`main.py`) was generated.
- Dependencies are installed only in the phase that needs them: pdfplumber added in Phase 1; OpenAI SDK deferred to Phase 2.
- Phase 1: `uv_build` build backend with `module-name = "credit_intel"` (copied from `uv init --package` output for uv 0.12.6) so the src-layout package is installed into the venv and importable by tests.
- PRD v0.1 key decisions: CLI-only Release 1; text-based PDFs only (no OCR); two annual periods; LLM extracts but never calculates; evidence quotes verified deterministically on the cited page; `Decimal` arithmetic; rules configured in TOML via stdlib `tomllib`; no composite risk score or credit decision; LLM self-reported confidence not used.
- **D-01 (2026-10-12): pdfplumber (MIT licence) for PDF text extraction.** Chosen over PyMuPDF (AGPL-3.0) to keep commercial use open. Wrapped behind a replaceable `TextExtractor` interface. Financial table extraction must be tested against representative PDFs in Phase 1. Installed in Phase 1 (0.11.10); transitive dependency licences confirmed — see Phase 1 "Dependencies added".
- **D-02 (2026-10-12): OpenAI API as initial LLM provider; `gpt-4o-mini` as proposed baseline model.** Availability, structured-output support, and pricing to be verified at the start of Phase 2. Provider and model are configurable. API key via `OPENAI_API_KEY` environment variable only, never in Git. Only public or synthetic financial data may be sent to the hosted API. No SDK installed, no `.env` created, and no API calls made yet.
